
[main/java/com/decho/cse/bridge/BaseDialect.java]
package com.decho.cse.bridge;
import hla.rti1516e.InteractionClassHandle;
import hla.rti1516e.ParameterHandleValueMap;
import org.slf4j.Logger;
import org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;
/**
* Plumbing shared by the tasking dialects: encoder lifecycle, handle lookup and
* parameter-map construction.
*/
abstract class BaseDialect implements TaskDialect {
protected final Logger logger = LoggerFactory.getLogger(getClass());
protected final BridgeFederate federate;
protected final Identity identity;
private volatile HlaEncoder encoder;
protected BaseDialect(BridgeFederate federate, Identity identity) {
this.federate = federate;
this.identity = identity;
}
/**
* The RTI encoder factory, created on first use. Doing this lazily rather
* than in the constructor keeps a classpath/RTI problem from failing Spring
* startup, which would take the /health and /status endpoints down with it
* and hide the actual error.
*/
protected HlaEncoder enc() throws Exception {
HlaEncoder local = encoder;
if (local == null) {
synchronized (this) {
local = encoder;
if (local == null) {
local = new HlaEncoder();
encoder = local;
}
}
}
return local;
}
/** @throws IllegalStateException if the class is not in the loaded FOM */
protected InteractionClassHandle interaction(String className) {
return federate.interaction(className);
}
protected ParameterHandleValueMap newParams(int capacity) throws Exception {
return federate.newParameterMap(capacity);
}
/** Set a parameter the FOM marks Required. Fails loudly if it is missing. */
protected void put(ParameterHandleValueMap params,
InteractionClassHandle handle,
String parameter,
byte[] value) throws Exception {
params.put(federate.parameter(handle, parameter), value);
}
/**
* Set a parameter the FOM marks Optional, skipping it if this particular FOM
* does not define it.
*
* <p>Needed because the bridge may be talking to a FOM assembled from a
* different set of modules than the reference one: the NETN-BASE root
* parameters ({@code UniqueId}, {@code SendTime}) exist only if NETN-BASE is
* loaded, and a federation can legitimately define a task class without
* them. Omitting an optional parameter means "use the FOM's default", which
* is exactly what we want; failing the whole task because an optional
* parameter is absent is not.
*/
protected void putOptional(ParameterHandleValueMap params,
InteractionClassHandle handle,
String parameter,
byte[] value) {
try {
params.put(federate.parameter(handle, parameter), value);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("Optional parameter {} not present in the loaded FOM, omitting: {}",
parameter, e.getMessage());
}
}
protected void send(InteractionClassHandle handle, ParameterHandleValueMap params)
throws Exception {
federate.sendInteraction(handle, params);
}
}

[main/java/com/decho/cse/bridge/BridgeApplication.java]
package com.decho.cse.bridge;
import org.slf4j.Logger;
import org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;
import org.slf4j.bridge.SLF4JBridgeHandler;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.ObjectProvider;
import org.springframework.boot.SpringApplication;
import org.springframework.boot.info.BuildProperties;
import org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.SpringBootApplication;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import java.util.logging.Level;
import java.util.logging.LogManager;
/**
* CSE HLA Bridge — Spring Boot entry point.
*
* Starts:
*   1. Spring Boot REST API on port 8080
*   2. BridgeFederate in a virtual thread (connects to Pitch RTI via FedPro)
*
* The bridge retries connection until the federation is running.
*/
@SpringBootApplication
public class BridgeApplication {
private static final Logger log = LoggerFactory.getLogger(BridgeApplication.class);
public static void main(String[] args) {
// se.pitch.oss.fedpro logs via java.util.logging (JUL), not SLF4J.
// Bridge JUL records into SLF4J/Logback so
// `logging.level.se.pitch.oss.fedpro` in application.properties has
// something to control, giving visibility into the FedPro client's
// internal connection/session handling. jul-to-slf4j is already on
// the classpath transitively via spring-boot-starter-web/-logging.
LogManager.getLogManager().reset();
SLF4JBridgeHandler.install();
// Raise the JUL logger's own threshold too — installing the bridge
// handler alone doesn't change what level of records JUL will even
// create; without this, FINE/FINEST records never reach the handler.
java.util.logging.Logger.getLogger("se.pitch.oss.fedpro").setLevel(Level.FINEST);
SpringApplication.run(BridgeApplication.class, args);
}
/**
* The build this jar came from, resolved once at startup.
*
* <p>Exists because a deployed bridge whose behaviour matches no commit in the
* repository cannot be debugged: every conclusion drawn from reading the source
* is unfounded, and there is no way to tell whether a fix actually shipped. The
* value is logged here and served on {@code GET /status}, because a line
* printed at startup is not much use on a pod that has been running for hours.
*
* <p>{@code sha} comes from {@code -Dgit.sha} at build time. "unknown" means
* the image was built outside the documented Docker path, which is itself worth
* knowing.
*/
@Bean
public BuildInfo buildInfo(ObjectProvider<BuildProperties> buildProperties) {
BuildProperties properties = buildProperties.getIfAvailable();
BuildInfo info = properties == null
? new BuildInfo("unknown", "unknown", "unknown")
: new BuildInfo(
properties.getVersion(),
properties.get("sha") == null ? "unknown" : properties.get("sha"),
properties.getTime() == null ? "unknown" : properties.getTime().toString());
if (properties == null) {
log.warn("No build-info.properties on the classpath, so this bridge cannot say which "
+ "commit it was built from. Builds through the project Dockerfile always "
+ "carry one; an image without it was built some other way.");
} else {
log.info("Build: version={} sha={} builtAt={}", info.version(), info.sha(), info.builtAt());
}
return info;
}
/** Version, commit and build time of the running jar. */
public record BuildInfo(String version, String sha, String builtAt) {}
@Bean
public EntityStateStore entityStateStore() {
return new EntityStateStore();
}
@Bean
public BridgeFederate bridgeFederate(
EntityStateStore store,
BridgeDiagnostics diagnostics,
@Value("${bridge.cse-environment}")                String cseEnvironment,
@Value("${bridge.crc-address}")                    String crcAddressOverride,
@Value("${bridge.fedpro-address}")                 String fedproAddressOverride,
@Value("${bridge.fedpro-protocol}")                String fedproProtocol,
@Value("${bridge.fedpro-sni-hostname}")             String fedproSniHostname,
@Value("${bridge.lrc-advertised-address}")          String lrcAdvertisedAddress,
@Value("${bridge.fedpro-connect-timeout-seconds}")  int fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds,
@Value("${bridge.federation-name}")       String federationName,
@Value("${bridge.fom-path}")              String fomPath,
@Value("${bridge.mim-path}")              String mimPath,
@Value("${bridge.retry-attempts}")        int retryAttempts,
@Value("${bridge.retry-delay-seconds}")   int retryDelay,
@Value("${bridge.port-probe-enabled}")    boolean portProbeEnabled,
@Value("${bridge.time-management-enabled}") boolean timeManagementEnabled,
@Value("${bridge.tasker-callsign}")      String taskerCallsign,
@Value("${bridge.tasker-uuid}")          String taskerUuid,
@Value("${bridge.mrm-federate}")         String mrmFederate,
@Value("${bridge.task-dialect}")         String taskDialect,
@Value("${bridge.federate-name}")        String federateName,
@Value("${bridge.mode}")                 String mode,
@Value("${bridge.create-federation}")    boolean createFederation,
@Value("${bridge.auto-connect}")         boolean autoConnect,
@Value("${bridge.subscribe-base-entity}") boolean subscribeBaseEntity,
@Value("${bridge.attribute-refresh-seconds}") int attributeRefreshSeconds,
@Value("${bridge.attribute-refresh-max-requests}") int attributeRefreshMaxRequests
) {
// CSE_ENVIRONMENT derives both addresses from the
// 30{ENVIRONMENT}{APPLICATION} port pattern — one value instead of
// computing exact ports by hand. Explicit FEDPRO_ADDRESS/CRC_ADDRESS
// env vars, if set, always win over the derived value. See
// EnvironmentPorts.java.
String fedproAddress = EnvironmentPorts.resolveFedproAddress(cseEnvironment, fedproProtocol, fedproAddressOverride);
String crcAddress = EnvironmentPorts.resolveCrcAddress(cseEnvironment, crcAddressOverride);
// The tasking identity is dialect dependent: v2 records the Tasker as a
// Callsign string, v4 as a UUID reference to the commanding entity. Both
// are supplied and each dialect uses the one its FOM defines.
TaskDialect.Identity taskIdentity =
new TaskDialect.Identity(taskerCallsign, taskerUuid, mrmFederate);
BridgeFederate federate = new BridgeFederate(
crcAddress, fedproAddress, fedproProtocol, fedproSniHostname,
lrcAdvertisedAddress, fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds, federationName, fomPath, mimPath,
timeManagementEnabled, store, diagnostics, taskIdentity, taskDialect,
federateName, mode, createFederation, subscribeBaseEntity, attributeRefreshSeconds,
attributeRefreshMaxRequests
);
log.info("Starting HLA bridge — CSE_ENVIRONMENT={} CRC: {} FedPro: {} (protocol={}) Federation: {}",
cseEnvironment, crcAddress, fedproAddress, fedproProtocol, federationName);
// Both of these decide what this instance *is*, and both are the first
// thing you want to know when two bridges are joined to one federation:
// which name it took, and whether it can task or only watch.
log.info("Federate name: {} (a suffix is appended if that name is already in use). "
+ "Mode: {}.", federateName,
federate.isMonitorOnly()
? "monitor-only — subscribes to entity state, publishes nothing, tasks nothing"
: "full — monitoring and tasking");
log.info("Tasking dialect preference: {} (the loaded FOM decides when 'auto').",
taskDialect == null || taskDialect.isBlank() ? "auto" : taskDialect);
// Whether an absent federation is an error or an invitation. Creating one
// is how a bridge ends up connected, healthy-looking and alone, so the
// policy in force is worth stating before the first connection attempt
// rather than inferring it afterwards from a warning that may not appear.
log.info("Absent federation policy: {}.", createFederation
? "CREATE it (BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION=true) — only correct when no simulator is "
+ "expected to be running, since anyone joining later must merge with our FOM"
: "wait for it (BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION=false) — the connect attempt fails and is "
+ "retried until the simulators' own federation exists");
log.info("Will retry {} times with {}s delay", retryAttempts, retryDelay);
// Whether this process takes a pRTI licence seat the moment it starts, or
// only when asked. Worth stating up front for the same reason as the
// federation policy above: a bridge sitting deliberately idle and a bridge
// failing to find its federation both report connected=false, and only the
// startup log distinguishes them.
log.info("Startup connection policy: {}.", autoConnect
? "CONNECT now (BRIDGE_AUTO_CONNECT=true) — join as soon as the federation exists "
+ "and hold a seat from then on"
: "WAIT (BRIDGE_AUTO_CONNECT=false) — serve the REST API and idle the run loop with "
+ "connected=false, taking no seat until a RECONNECT command arrives");
// Connect in background virtual thread — don't block Spring startup.
// The REST API returns connected=false until the federation is up, and
// indefinitely when auto-connect is off.
Thread.ofVirtual().name("hla-bridge").start(() -> {
try {
if (portProbeEnabled) {
// Optional startup diagnostic — see PortProbe.java. Runs
// from this pod's own network position, which is the
// only vantage point that can meaningfully test
// connectivity to the CSE-Prep firewall/ports.
// Environment-aware so switching CSE_ENVIRONMENT doesn't
// leave this probing the wrong environment's ports.
String probeHost = fedproAddress.contains(":")
? fedproAddress.substring(0, fedproAddress.indexOf(':'))
: fedproAddress;
int envNumber = EnvironmentPorts.parseEnvironmentNumber(cseEnvironment);
String probeSni = String.format(
"pitch-federate-protocol-server.cse-prep-simulation-%02d.apps.cse-cluster.cse.local",
envNumber);
PortProbe.runAndLog(log, probeHost, envNumber, probeSni);
}
if (autoConnect) {
federate.connectWithRetry(retryAttempts, retryDelay);
} else {
log.info("Auto-connect is off — not joining. The run loop starts idle and no "
+ "pRTI seat is held. POST /reconnect, or send a RECONNECT command on "
+ "the command stream, to connect and join.");
}
// Started in both cases, and deliberately so. The loop is what
// services requestRejoin(), so skipping it when auto-connect is off
// would leave a bridge that can never be told to connect —
// /reconnect would answer "the run loop is not running".
federate.run(); // blocks until stopped
} catch (Exception e) {
log.error("HLA bridge fatal error: {}", e.getMessage(), e);
store.setConnected(false);
}
});
return federate;
}
}

[main/java/com/decho/cse/bridge/BridgeDiagnostics.java]
package com.decho.cse.bridge;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.ArrayDeque;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Comparator;
import java.util.Deque;
import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Optional;
import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong;
/**
* What the bridge knows about its own state, in a form that can be read over
* HTTP instead of inferred from pod logs.
*
* <h2>Why this exists</h2>
* Every hard problem on this bridge so far was diagnosable only by reading logs
* and guessing:
*
* <ul>
*   <li>29 of 33 interaction classes were absent from the federation's FOM. The
*       only symptom was a 409 per endpoint; the startup log said so once and
*       scrolled away.</li>
*   <li>Tasks were reported as sent while nothing acted on them, because "the
*       interaction left the bridge" and "a federate accepted it" are different
*       facts and only the first was visible.</li>
*   <li>Whether the other federate was publishing came from the RTI's own MOM
*       data, which the bridge decoded, logged once, and threw away.</li>
* </ul>
*
* <p>So this class keeps the small amount of state needed to answer "why isn't
* this working?", and {@link #assess} turns it into ordered findings rather than
* leaving the reader to correlate five endpoints by hand.
*
* <p>Everything here is observational: it records what happened and never
* changes what the bridge does, so a wrong diagnosis cannot break tasking.
* Bounded on purpose — a fixed-size task ring buffer and one counter per
* interaction class, because this runs in a 512 MB container beside the RTI.
*/
@Component
public class BridgeDiagnostics {
/** How many recent tasks to keep. Enough for a debugging session. */
private static final int TASK_HISTORY = 50;
/** Severity of a {@link Finding}. */
public enum Level { OK, INFO, WARN, ERROR }
/**
* One conclusion about the current state.
*
* @param summary what is true
* @param detail  why it matters, or what to do about it
*/
public record Finding(String level, String summary, String detail) {}
/**
* An interaction class the bridge knows about, and what it can do with it.
*
* @param dialect  "v2", "v4" or "rpr"
* @param module   the FOM module that defines it — how to tell which modules
*                 the federation was created with
* @param resolved whether the loaded FOM defines it at all
*/
public record FomClassStatus(String name, String dialect, String module,
boolean resolved, boolean published, boolean subscribed) {}
/** A task this bridge sent, and anything that came back about it. */
public static final class TaskRecord {
private final String taskId;
private final String operation;
private final String fomClass;
private final String dialect;
private final String entity;
private final String sentAt;
private final List<String> acknowledgements =
java.util.Collections.synchronizedList(new ArrayList<>());
TaskRecord(String taskId, String operation, String fomClass, String dialect,
String entity, Instant sentAt) {
this.taskId    = taskId;
this.operation = operation;
this.fomClass  = fomClass;
this.dialect   = dialect;
this.entity    = entity;
this.sentAt    = sentAt.toString();
}
public String getTaskId()                 { return taskId; }
public String getOperation()              { return operation; }
public String getFomClass()               { return fomClass; }
public String getDialect()                { return dialect; }
public String getEntity()                 { return entity; }
public String getSentAt()                 { return sentAt; }
public List<String> getAcknowledgements() { return acknowledgements; }
/** The question that actually matters: did anything answer? */
public boolean isAcknowledged() { return !acknowledgements.isEmpty(); }
}
/** A federate joined to this federation, as reported by the RTI's MOM. */
public record FederateRecord(String name, String type, String host,
Integer objectsRegistered, Integer updatesSent,
List<String> fomModules, String lastReportedAt) {}
/** How many of an interaction class arrived, and when the last one did. */
public record InteractionTraffic(String fomClass, long count, String lastReceivedAt) {}
/**
* The result of trying to subscribe to one object class.
*
* <p>This is the accounting that was previously only visible as DEBUG log lines,
* and its absence is why "why is this field null?" took a stream query and two
* log dumps to answer. An entity arrives with a name and nothing else when the
* class it was published as resolved to nothing, or when the attributes carrying
* its identity are spelled differently in the FOM actually loaded — and both are
* recorded here, per class, by name.
*
* @param subscribed attribute names this FOM defined and we asked for
* @param missing    attribute names this FOM did not define, skipped silently
*/
public record ObjectClassStatus(String name, boolean resolved,
List<String> subscribed, List<String> missing) {}
// ── State ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
private final Map<String, FomClassStatus> fomClasses = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final Map<String, ObjectClassStatus> objectClasses = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final Deque<TaskRecord> tasks = new ArrayDeque<>();
private final Map<String, TaskRecord> tasksById = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final Map<String, AtomicLong> receivedCounts = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final Map<String, String> receivedLast = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final Map<String, FederateRecord> federates = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
/** Entity id to the control actions it advertises. Empty list = advertises none. */
private final Map<String, List<String>> supportedActions = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final AtomicLong acknowledgementsReceived = new AtomicLong();
private final AtomicLong tasksSent = new AtomicLong();
private volatile String dialectId = "unknown";
private volatile String dialectName = "unknown";
private volatile String dialectPreference = "auto";
private volatile String connectedSince;
private volatile String lastConnectionError;
// Identity of the current connection. Recorded because two bridges with
// identical configuration can end up in different federations of the same
// name — the CRC address is not stable here, so each instance learns its own
// working candidate — and without these, one bridge seeing 300 entities and
// another seeing none looks like a bug in the one that sees none.
private volatile String federateName;
private volatile String crcCandidate;
private volatile boolean federationCreatedByUs;
// ── Recording ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
/** Called once per known class after handle resolution. */
void recordFomClass(String name, String dialect, boolean resolved) {
fomClasses.put(name,
new FomClassStatus(name, dialect, moduleOf(name), resolved, false, false));
}
/** Called once per object class after the attempt to subscribe to it. */
void recordObjectClass(String name, boolean resolved,
List<String> subscribed, List<String> missing) {
objectClasses.put(name, new ObjectClassStatus(name, resolved,
List.copyOf(subscribed), List.copyOf(missing)));
}
void recordPublished(String name) {
fomClasses.computeIfPresent(name, (key, old) -> new FomClassStatus(
old.name(), old.dialect(), old.module(), old.resolved(), true, old.subscribed()));
}
void recordSubscribed(String name) {
fomClasses.computeIfPresent(name, (key, old) -> new FomClassStatus(
old.name(), old.dialect(), old.module(), old.resolved(), old.published(), true));
}
void recordDialect(String id, String name, String preference) {
this.dialectId         = id;
this.dialectName       = name;
this.dialectPreference = preference;
}
/**
* The identity of this connection: the name actually joined under, the CRC
* candidate that answered, and whether this bridge created the execution.
*
* <p>Called once per successful join. All three answer "where am I?", which is
* a different question from "what can I see?" and has to be settled first: a
* bridge alone in an execution of its own making presents identically to one
* with a subscription gap.
*/
void recordIdentity(String federateName, String crcCandidate, boolean federationCreatedByUs) {
this.federateName          = federateName;
this.crcCandidate          = crcCandidate;
this.federationCreatedByUs = federationCreatedByUs;
}
void recordConnected() {
this.connectedSince      = Instant.now().toString();
this.lastConnectionError = null;
}
void recordConnectionError(String message) {
this.lastConnectionError = Instant.now() + " — " + message;
}
/** Called when the bridge sends a task, whether or not anything receives it. */
void recordTaskSent(String taskId, String operation, String fomClass,
String dialect, String entity) {
TaskRecord record =
new TaskRecord(taskId, operation, fomClass, dialect, entity, Instant.now());
tasksSent.incrementAndGet();
synchronized (tasks) {
tasks.addFirst(record);
while (tasks.size() > TASK_HISTORY) {
tasksById.remove(tasks.removeLast().getTaskId());
}
}
tasksById.put(taskId, record);
}
/**
* Called for anything referencing a task — an ETR task status, a
* TaskStatusReport or an SMC_Response.
*
* <p>Unmatched acknowledgements are counted but not attributed: they refer to
* tasks issued by another federate, which is useful to know (someone else is
* tasking, so the classes definitely work) but not ours.
*/
void recordAcknowledgement(String taskOrActionId, String description) {
acknowledgementsReceived.incrementAndGet();
TaskRecord record = taskOrActionId == null ? null : tasksById.get(taskOrActionId);
if (record != null) {
record.acknowledgements.add(Instant.now() + " — " + description);
}
}
void recordInteractionReceived(String fomClass) {
receivedCounts.computeIfAbsent(fomClass, key -> new AtomicLong()).incrementAndGet();
receivedLast.put(fomClass, Instant.now().toString());
}
void recordFederate(String name, String type, String host,
Integer objectsRegistered, Integer updatesSent, List<String> fomModules) {
String key = name == null || name.isBlank() ? "(unnamed)" : name;
federates.put(key, new FederateRecord(key, type, host, objectsRegistered, updatesSent,
fomModules == null ? List.of() : List.copyOf(fomModules),
Instant.now().toString()));
}
void recordSupportedActions(String entityId, List<String> actions) {
supportedActions.put(entityId, List.copyOf(actions));
}
/**
* Reset per-connection state on reconnect, so counts and class lists describe
* the federation we are attached to now.
*
* <p>Task history is deliberately kept: what was attempted before a
* reconnect is exactly what someone debugging wants to see.
*/
void clearConnectionState() {
fomClasses.clear();
objectClasses.clear();
federates.clear();
supportedActions.clear();
receivedCounts.clear();
receivedLast.clear();
dialectId      = "unknown";
dialectName    = "unknown";
connectedSince = null;
// Identity belongs to a connection, not to the process: a reconnect can
// land on a different CRC candidate, and reporting the old one would be
// worse than reporting none.
federateName          = null;
crcCandidate          = null;
federationCreatedByUs = false;
}
// ── Reading ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
/** Object-class subscription results, in FOM class-name order. */
public List<ObjectClassStatus> objectClassStatuses() {
List<ObjectClassStatus> all = new ArrayList<>(objectClasses.values());
all.sort(Comparator.comparing(ObjectClassStatus::name));
return all;
}
public List<FomClassStatus> fomClassStatuses() {
List<FomClassStatus> all = new ArrayList<>(fomClasses.values());
all.sort(Comparator.comparing(FomClassStatus::dialect)
.thenComparing(FomClassStatus::module)
.thenComparing(FomClassStatus::name));
return all;
}
public List<TaskRecord> recentTasks() {
synchronized (tasks) {
return new ArrayList<>(tasks);
}
}
public List<FederateRecord> federateRecords() {
List<FederateRecord> all = new ArrayList<>(federates.values());
all.sort(Comparator.comparing(FederateRecord::name));
return all;
}
public List<InteractionTraffic> interactionTraffic() {
List<InteractionTraffic> all = new ArrayList<>();
receivedCounts.forEach((name, count) ->
all.add(new InteractionTraffic(name, count.get(), receivedLast.get(name))));
all.sort(Comparator.comparingLong(InteractionTraffic::count).reversed());
return all;
}
public Optional<List<String>> supportedActionsFor(String entityId) {
return Optional.ofNullable(supportedActions.get(entityId));
}
public long tasksSent()                { return tasksSent.get(); }
public long acknowledgementsReceived() { return acknowledgementsReceived.get(); }
public String connectedSince()         { return connectedSince; }
public String lastConnectionError()    { return lastConnectionError; }
/** The name this bridge joined under, or null before a successful join. */
public String federateName()           { return federateName; }
/** The CRC candidate that answered, or null before a successful connect. */
public String crcCandidate()           { return crcCandidate; }
/** Whether this bridge created the execution it is joined to. */
public boolean federationCreatedByUs() { return federationCreatedByUs; }
/**
* Federates known from the RTI's MOM, including this bridge.
*
* <p>Zero means the RTI has not reported any yet, not that the federation is
* empty — MOM attributes are requested once at connect.
*/
public int federateCount()             { return federates.size(); }
/** Whether a MOM record describes this bridge rather than another federate. */
private boolean isUs(FederateRecord record) {
return federateName != null && federateName.equals(record.name());
}
/** How long the current connection has been up. */
public String uptime() {
String since = connectedSince;
if (since == null) {
return "not connected";
}
try {
Duration up = Duration.between(Instant.parse(since), Instant.now());
return up.toHours() + "h " + up.toMinutesPart() + "m " + up.toSecondsPart() + "s";
} catch (Exception e) {
return since;
}
}
// ── Assessment ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
/**
* Turn the state above into ordered findings, most serious first.
*
* <p>The rules encode what this project learned the hard way: a resolved
* handle is not a listening federate, a 200 from the REST layer is not an
* accepted task, and an absent status class means an accepted task cannot be
* distinguished from an ignored one.
*
* @param entitiesWithoutUniqueId entities discovered with no NETN UniqueId,
*                                which therefore cannot be tasked
*/
public List<Finding> assess(boolean connected, String federationName,
int entityCount, int entitiesWithoutUniqueId) {
List<Finding> findings = new ArrayList<>();
if (!connected) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.ERROR.name(), "Not connected to the RTI.",
lastConnectionError == null
? "The bridge is retrying. Nothing can be monitored or tasked until it "
+ "joins. Check CSE_ENVIRONMENT / FEDPRO_ADDRESS and the egress policy."
: "Last error: " + lastConnectionError));
return findings;
}
findings.add(new Finding(Level.OK.name(),
"Connected to federation '" + federationName + "' for " + uptime() + "."
+ (federateName == null ? "" : " Joined as '" + federateName + "'"
+ (crcCandidate == null ? "" : " via CRC candidate '" + crcCandidate + "'")
+ "."), ""));
// ── Am I in the right federation at all? ──
// This block comes first because everything after it describes what can be
// seen from wherever the bridge happens to be, and is worthless if that is
// the wrong place. "connected: true, entities: 0" has several causes and
// the structural ones are the cheapest to rule out: an execution of our
// own making, or one nobody else has joined. Eliminating those here is
// what makes the remaining explanations — nothing is publishing, or a
// genuine subscription gap — worth spending time on, and in that order.
// See "Reading bridge_get_federates" in DIAGNOSTICS.md.
if (federationCreatedByUs) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.ERROR.name(),
"This bridge CREATED federation '" + federationName + "' — it was not already "
+ "running.",
"So every other federate is somewhere else, and nothing will ever be discovered "
+ "here however the subscriptions look. Either the simulators are not running, "
+ "or this bridge reached a different CRC than they did (candidate '"
+ crcCandidate + "'). Set BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION=false and the bridge will "
+ "wait for the real federation instead of standing in for it."));
}
long otherFederates = federates.values().stream().filter(record -> !isUs(record)).count();
if (federates.isEmpty()) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.INFO.name(),
"The RTI has not reported any federate records yet.",
"Not evidence of an empty federation: MOM attributes are requested once at "
+ "connect, so anything that joined at another moment is simply unreported. "
+ "POST /diagnostics/refresh-federates, then read /diagnostics/federates."));
} else if (otherFederates == 0) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.ERROR.name(),
"This bridge is the ONLY federate in '" + federationName + "'.",
"From the RTI's own MOM accounting, so it is not a guess: no simulator is "
+ "joined to the federation this bridge is attached to. Nothing about "
+ "subscriptions or the FOM can explain the missing entities, and changing "
+ "either will not help. Compare CRC candidate ('" + crcCandidate + "'), "
+ "FEDERATION_NAME and CSE_ENVIRONMENT against a bridge that does see "
+ "entities."));
} else {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.OK.name(),
otherFederates + " other federate(s) joined to '" + federationName + "'.",
"See /diagnostics/federates for who they are and whether they are publishing."));
}
// ── Tasking dialect ──
if ("none".equals(dialectId) || "unknown".equals(dialectId)) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.ERROR.name(),
"No NETN tasking dialect is available.",
"This FOM defines neither ETR_Root.ETR_Task.* (v2-era) nor "
+ "SMC_EntityControl.Task.* (v4), so every tasking endpoint returns 409. "
+ "Entities can still be monitored. See /diagnostics/fom for what it does "
+ "define."));
} else {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.OK.name(), "Tasking dialect: " + dialectName + ".",
"Detected from the loaded FOM; preference is '" + dialectPreference + "'."));
}
// ── Modules the federation did not load ──
// Only for the dialect in use: the other dialect's classes are *expected*
// to be absent, and listing them is what made the old log unreadable.
Map<String, List<String>> missingByModule = new LinkedHashMap<>();
for (FomClassStatus status : fomClasses.values()) {
boolean relevant = status.dialect().equals(dialectId) || "rpr".equals(status.dialect());
if (relevant && !status.resolved()) {
missingByModule.computeIfAbsent(status.module(), key -> new ArrayList<>())
.add(shortName(status.name()));
}
}
missingByModule.forEach((module, classes) -> findings.add(new Finding(Level.WARN.name(),
module + " is not loaded in this federation.",
"Endpoints needing these classes return 409: " + classes)));
// ── Entities ──
if (entityCount == 0) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.WARN.name(), "No entities discovered.",
"Either nothing is publishing, or it publishes object classes this bridge does "
+ "not subscribe to. Compare with /diagnostics/federates: a federate reporting "
+ "objectsRegistered > 0 while this is empty is a subscription gap on our side, "
+ "not a publishing problem on theirs."));
} else {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.OK.name(),
entityCount + " entity(ies) discovered.", ""));
}
if (entitiesWithoutUniqueId > 0) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.WARN.name(),
entitiesWithoutUniqueId + " entity(ies) have no NETN UniqueId.",
"NETN tasks address entities by UUID, so these cannot be tasked unless the UUID "
+ "is passed directly. They are probably published as plain RPR classes rather "
+ "than NETN_* ones."));
}
// ── Taskability, straight from the entities ──
if (supportedActions.isEmpty()) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.INFO.name(),
"No entity has published SupportedActions.",
"v4 entities may advertise which control actions their simulator accepts. "
+ "v2-era FOMs have no such attribute, so this is expected there. On v4 it "
+ "means taskability cannot be confirmed in advance — send a task and watch "
+ "for an acknowledgement."));
} else {
supportedActions.forEach((entityId, actions) -> findings.add(actions.isEmpty()
? new Finding(Level.WARN.name(),
"Entity " + entityId + " advertises no control actions.",
"Its simulator accepts no NETN entity control actions, so it cannot be "
+ "tasked whatever the FOM defines — a publisher-only federate as far as "
+ "tasking is concerned.")
: new Finding(Level.OK.name(),
"Entity " + entityId + " accepts: " + actions + ".",
"Published by the entity itself via SupportedActions.")));
}
// ── The question the REST layer cannot answer ──
long sent = tasksSent.get();
long acked = acknowledgementsReceived.get();
boolean feedbackAvailable = fomClasses.values().stream()
.anyMatch(status -> status.resolved() && isFeedbackClass(status.name()));
if (sent == 0) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.INFO.name(), "No tasks sent yet this session.", ""));
} else if (acked > 0) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.OK.name(),
acked + " acknowledgement(s) for " + sent + " task(s) sent.",
"See /diagnostics/tasks for which tasks were answered and how."));
} else if (feedbackAvailable) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.WARN.name(),
sent + " task(s) sent, none acknowledged.",
"The interactions left this bridge but no federate answered. A task status "
+ "class does exist in this FOM, and a NETN-SMC compliant federate must reply "
+ "to every entity control action — so the likeliest explanation is that "
+ "nothing subscribes to these task classes. A 200 from the REST layer only "
+ "means the interaction was sent."));
} else {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.WARN.name(),
sent + " task(s) sent, and this FOM has no task status class.",
"Without ETR_TaskStatus / TaskStatusReport / SMC_Response there is no way to "
+ "distinguish an accepted task from an ignored one. Judge by observed effect: "
+ "MagicMove is the clearest test, being an unconditional teleport with no "
+ "route planning or ROE involved."));
}
// ── Are we hearing anything at all? ──
if (receivedCounts.isEmpty()) {
findings.add(new Finding(Level.INFO.name(), "No interactions received at all.",
"Normal in a quiet scenario — reports and weapon events only appear when "
+ "something happens. Entity position updates are attribute reflections, "
+ "counted separately, and are the better sign of a healthy connection."));
}
return findings;
}
/** The classes that tell us whether a task was accepted. */
private static boolean isFeedbackClass(String name) {
return name.endsWith(".TaskStatusReport")
|| name.endsWith(".ETR_TaskStatus")
|| name.endsWith(".SMC_Response")
|| name.endsWith(".Response");
}
/**
* Which FOM module defines a class, derived from its name.
*
* <p>Kept here rather than declared per class in the dialects: this is
* observational metadata for humans, and deriving it means adding a class to
* a dialect cannot forget to declare it.
*/
private static String moduleOf(String className) {
if (className.contains("ETR_Root") || className.contains("ETR_Report")
|| className.contains("ETR_TaskStatus") || className.contains("ETR_SensorEvent")) {
return "NETN-ETR";
}
if (className.contains("MRM_Interaction")) {
return "NETN-MRM";
}
if (className.endsWith("SMC_EntityControl.Aggregate")
|| className.endsWith("SMC_EntityControl.Disaggregate")
|| className.endsWith("SMC_EntityControl.Divide")
|| className.endsWith("SMC_EntityControl.Merge")) {
return "NETN-MRM";
}
if (className.endsWith("Task.CreateObstacle") || className.endsWith("Task.ClearEngineering")) {
return "NETN-SE";
}
if (className.endsWith("Task.DisruptCommunication")) {
return "NETN-COM";
}
if (className.endsWith("SMC_EntityControl.SetSuppliesStatus")) {
return "NETN-LOG";
}
if (className.endsWith("SMC_Response")) {
return "NETN-SMC";
}
if (className.contains("SMC_EntityControl")) {
return "NETN-ETR";
}
return "RPR-FOM";
}
private static String shortName(String fomClassName) {
int lastDot = fomClassName.lastIndexOf('.');
return lastDot < 0 ? fomClassName : fomClassName.substring(lastDot + 1);
}
}

[main/java/com/decho/cse/bridge/BridgeFederate.java]
package com.decho.cse.bridge;
import hla.rti1516e.*;
import hla.rti1516e.exceptions.*;
import hla.rti1516e.time.HLAfloat64Interval;
import hla.rti1516e.time.HLAfloat64TimeFactory;
import jakarta.annotation.PreDestroy;
import org.slf4j.Logger;
import org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;
import com.decho.cse.bridge.model.Entity;
import java.io.File;
import java.net.URL;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;
public class BridgeFederate {
private static final Logger logger = LoggerFactory.getLogger(BridgeFederate.class);
// ── FOM class names ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// These are the names as declared in the FOM actually loaded into the
// federation (cse-hla-bridge/foms/SWORD_FOM.xml, which is composed from the
// NETN FOM modules mirrored in NETN-FOM-master/).
//
// The previous version of this class asked for "HLAinteractionRoot.CseEvent.*"
// (CreateEntity/UpdateEntity/DeleteEntity/StartScenario/PauseScenario/
// StopScenario). No such classes exist in any NETN or RPR FOM module — they
// appear to have been placeholders — so handle resolution failed on the
// first call and the bridge never got past initializeHandles().
//
// The real model: tasking is a tree of *concrete* interaction subclasses
// under ETR_Root.ETR_Task, disambiguated by class, not by a "TaskType"
// string parameter. ETR_Root, ETR_Task, ETR_SimCon and ETR_TaskManagement
// are all abstract (sharing=Neither) and cannot be published or sent.
//
// These are the NETN v2-era names. The v4 equivalents live in
// SmcV4Dialect, next to the code that encodes for them — see TaskDialect
// for why both are supported. Only the RPR-FOM and MOM names below are
// dialect independent.
public static final class Fom {
private static final String TASK   = "HLAinteractionRoot.ETR_Root.ETR_Task.";
private static final String SIMCON = "HLAinteractionRoot.ETR_Root.ETR_SimCon.";
private static final String TASKMGT= "HLAinteractionRoot.ETR_Root.ETR_TaskManagement.";
private static final String REPORT = "HLAinteractionRoot.ETR_Root.ETR_Report.";
private static final String MRM    = "HLAinteractionRoot.MRM_Interaction.";
// Tasks (ETR_Task subclasses) — inherit TaskId, Taskee, Tasker,
// StartWhen, Why, TaskMode, CommunicationNetworkIds.
public static final String MOVE_TO_LOCATION      = TASK + "MoveToLocation";
public static final String MOVE_TO_ENTITY        = TASK + "MoveToEntity";
public static final String PATROL                = TASK + "Patrol";
public static final String PATROL_REPEATING      = TASK + "Patrol.PatrolRepeating";
public static final String FIRE_AT_LOCATION      = TASK + "FireAtLocation";
public static final String FIRE_AT_ENTITY        = TASK + "FireAtEntity";
public static final String SET_RULES_OF_ENGAGEMENT = TASK + "SetRulesOfEngagement";
public static final String CREATE_OBSTACLE       = TASK + "CreateObstacle";
public static final String CLEAR_OBSTACLE        = TASK + "ClearObstacle";
public static final String DISRUPT_COMMUNICATION = TASK + "DisruptCommunication";
public static final String STOP_AT_SIDE_OF_ROAD  = TASK + "StopAtSideOfRoad";
public static final String TURN_TO_HEADING       = TASK + "TurnToHeading";
public static final String SET_ORDERED_SPEED     = TASK + "SetOrderedSpeed";
public static final String SET_ORDERED_ALTITUDE  = TASK + "SetOrderedAltitude";
// Simulation control (ETR_SimCon subclasses) — "magic"/umpire actions.
// Inherit TaskId, Taskee, Tasker only (no StartWhen/TaskMode).
public static final String MAGIC_MOVE            = SIMCON + "MagicMove";
public static final String MAGIC_RESOURCE        = SIMCON + "MagicResource";
public static final String QUERY_CAPABILITIES    = SIMCON + "QuerySupportedCapabilities";
public static final String CAPABILITIES_SUPPORTED= SIMCON + "CapabilitiesSupported";
// Task management — inherit TaskManagementId, Taskee, Tasker.
public static final String CANCEL_ALL_TASKS      = TASKMGT + "CancelAllTasks";
public static final String CANCEL_SPECIFIED_TASKS= TASKMGT + "CancelSpecifiedTasks";
public static final String TASK_STATUS_REPORT    = TASKMGT + "TaskStatusReport";
// Reports published by the simulators.
public static final String POSITION_STATUS_REPORT   = REPORT + "StatusReport.PositionStatusReport";
public static final String DAMAGE_STATUS_REPORT     = REPORT + "StatusReport.DamageStatusReport";
public static final String RESOURCE_STATUS_REPORT   = REPORT + "StatusReport.ResourceStatusReport";
public static final String UNDER_ATTACK_REPORT      = REPORT + "StatusReport.UnderAttackStatusReport";
public static final String SPOT_REPORT              = REPORT + "SpotReport";
public static final String IN_WEAPON_RANGE_REPORT   = REPORT + "InWeaponRangeReport";
// Multi-resolution modelling (aggregate / disaggregate).
public static final String MRM_AGGREGATE     = MRM + "Request.Aggregate";
public static final String MRM_DISAGGREGATE  = MRM + "Request.Disaggregate";
public static final String MRM_RESPONSE      = MRM + "Response";
// RPR-FOM simulation management. There is no NETN "scenario control"
// interaction; start/pause/stop of an exercise is done with the
// DIS-derived StartResume / StopFreeze interactions.
public static final String START_RESUME = "HLAinteractionRoot.StartResume";
public static final String STOP_FREEZE  = "HLAinteractionRoot.StopFreeze";
public static final String WEAPON_FIRE         = "HLAinteractionRoot.WeaponFire";
public static final String MUNITION_DETONATION = "HLAinteractionRoot.MunitionDetonation";
/**
* MOM (Management Object Model) class the RTI itself publishes, with one
* instance per joined federate. Subscribing to it turns "is anyone else
* in this federation, and are they publishing?" from a trip to the CRC
* web UI into a log line.
*/
public static final String MOM_FEDERATE = "HLAobjectRoot.HLAmanager.HLAfederate";
private Fom() {}
}
/** Attributes wanted from the MOM federate class. */
private static final List<String> MOM_FEDERATE_ATTRIBUTES = List.of(
"HLAfederateName",
"HLAfederateType",
"HLAfederateHost",
// The FOM modules each federate joined with: the most direct answer
// to "which FOM is this federation actually running?".
"HLAFOMmoduleDesignatorList",
"HLAobjectInstancesRegistered",
"HLAupdatesSent");
/**
* Dialect-independent RPR-FOM simulation management this federate sends.
* Everything else it publishes comes from the selected {@link TaskDialect}.
*/
private static final List<String> RPR_PUBLISHED = List.of(
Fom.START_RESUME,
Fom.STOP_FREEZE
);
/** Dialect-independent RPR-FOM events this federate listens for. */
private static final List<String> RPR_SUBSCRIBED = List.of(
Fom.WEAPON_FIRE,
Fom.MUNITION_DETONATION
);
/**
* Object classes to subscribe to, with the attributes wanted from each.
*
* <p>The NETN {@code UniqueId} attribute — the UUID every ETR task needs as
* its {@code Taskee} — is declared on the NETN_* leaf classes, not on
* {@code Platform}. Subscribing only to {@code Platform} (as before) meant
* entities were discovered without a UUID, so nothing could ever be tasked.
*/
private static final Map<String, List<String>> SUBSCRIBED_OBJECTS = buildObjectSubscriptions();
private static Map<String, List<String>> buildObjectSubscriptions() {
// NETN v4 (NETN-Merged-With-RPR.xml, HLA Evolved) is the only model this
// bridge subscribes against. The v2-era layout it used to target is a
// different generation and the difference is not cosmetic:
//
//   v2: Callsign/Status/SymbolId/UniqueId live on NETN_* leaf classes
//       (NETN_GroundVehicle, NETN_Aggregate, ...)
//   v4: those classes do not exist. Callsign/Symbol/Status/SupportedActions
//       moved onto BaseEntity, aggregate attributes onto AggregateEntity,
//       SymbolId was renamed Symbol, and UniqueId moved to HLAobjectRoot
//       where the NETN MIM declares it.
//
// Subscribing with v2 names against a v4 federation fails silently — the
// class or attribute simply is not found, the subscription is skipped, and
// entities arrive with a position, a callsign and nothing else. That is the
// half-populated entity data this replaced.
Map<String, List<String>> map = new LinkedHashMap<>();
String physical = "HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity.PhysicalEntity.";
// UniqueId is inherited from HLAobjectRoot, where the NETN MIM
// (NETN-MIM.xml) declares it — it is not an attribute of BaseEntity or
// of any RPR class, which is why it has to be requested explicitly on
// whichever class an entity is actually discovered at.
// SupportedActions (NETN-SMC, v4 only) is how a NETN v4 entity advertises
// which control actions its simulator will accept. It answers "can this
// entity be tasked at all, and with what?" from the entity itself rather
// than by sending a task and waiting to see whether anything happens.
// Absent in v2-era FOMs, where subscribeObjectClass skips it silently.
// Everything a PhysicalEntity subclass can offer, across every generation.
//
// Both spellings of the identity attributes are listed deliberately. The
// three FOMs in play do not agree, and the names are case-sensitive:
//
//   netn2_2010.xml   (what SWORD's plugin loads)  UniqueID, Symbol
//   NETN3 / SWORD_FOM.xml                         UniqueId, SymbolId
//   NETN-Merged-With-RPR.xml (v4)                 Symbol; UniqueId via the MIM
//
// Asking for only one spelling is why UniqueId was always null: against
// netn2 the lookup throws NameNotFound and is skipped without comment, so
// no entity ever carries a UUID and nothing can be tasked. Listing both
// costs nothing — subscribeObjectClass resolves attributes one at a time
// and silently drops the ones this FOM does not define.
List<String> platformAttrs = List.of(
"UniqueId", "UniqueID", "Callsign", "Symbol", "SymbolId", "Status",
"SupportedActions",
"EntityIdentifier", "Spatial", "EntityType", "Marking",
"ForceIdentifier", "DamageState");
// Catch-all. BaseEntity is the root of every RPR entity class, so
// subscribing to it means an entity published as a class we have not
// enumerated is still discovered (as a BaseEntity) rather than being
// silently invisible. Discovery is what tells us something exists at
// all; the more specific subscriptions below add the useful attributes.
// In v4 these four are BaseEntity attributes, not leaf-class ones, so the
// catch-all is no longer just a discovery net — an object published as a
// class we have not enumerated still yields identity and position.
map.put("HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity", List.of(
"UniqueId", "UniqueID", "Callsign", "Symbol", "SymbolId", "Status",
"SupportedActions", "EntityType", "EntityIdentifier", "Spatial"));
// Physical entities, plain RPR classes and NETN leaves both.
//
// Which one an object is registered as depends on the federation's FOM
// generation, and subscribing to only one side is not a graceful
// degradation: the RTI then reports discovery at whatever ancestor we did
// subscribe to (BaseEntity), and every attribute declared below that point
// becomes unreachable. Subscribing to both means the object is discovered
// at its own class whichever generation is running.
// PhysicalEntity itself, not just its subclasses.
//
// Leaving this out was a real gap: an object registered as PhysicalEntity —
// or as one of the siblings below that we had not enumerated — has no
// subscribed ancestor nearer than BaseEntity, so the RTI reports discovery
// there and every attribute declared on PhysicalEntity becomes unreachable.
// DamageState, ForceIdentifier and Marking all live here, which is why they
// stayed null while Spatial and EntityType (BaseEntity attributes) did not.
// The observed signature was entityType "entity" on all 301 objects: the
// catch-all, reached because nothing more specific matched.
map.put(physical.substring(0, physical.length() - 1), platformAttrs);
map.put(physical + "Platform", platformAttrs);
map.put(physical + "Lifeform", platformAttrs);
// Non-platform physical entities, and the environmental branch. Cheap to
// add, skipped silently where absent, and each one removes a way for an
// object to fall back to the catch-all.
map.put("HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity.EnvironmentalEntity", platformAttrs);
for (String leaf : List.of(
"Sensor",
"CulturalFeature",
"Munition",
"Expendables",
"Radio",
"Supplies",
"Platform.Aircraft",
"Platform.AmphibiousVehicle",
"Platform.GroundVehicle",
"Platform.MultiDomainPlatform",
"Platform.Spacecraft",
"Platform.SubmersibleVessel",
"Platform.SurfaceVessel",
"Lifeform.Human",
"Lifeform.NonHuman",
// NETN leaves — present in netn2_2010 and NETN3, absent in v4,
// skipped silently where absent.
"Platform.Aircraft.NETN_Aircraft",
"Platform.AmphibiousVehicle.NETN_AmphibiousVehicle",
"Platform.GroundVehicle.NETN_GroundVehicle",
"Platform.MultiDomainPlatform.NETN_MultiDomainPlatform",
"Platform.Spacecraft.NETN_Spacecraft",
"Platform.SubmersibleVessel.NETN_SubmersibleVessel",
"Platform.SurfaceVessel.NETN_SurfaceVessel",
"Lifeform.Human.NETN_Human",
"Lifeform.NonHuman.NETN_NonHuman")) {
map.put(physical + leaf, platformAttrs);
}
// Aggregates — the class that actually matters here.
//
// SWORD runs with aggregate_mode=aggregate and its SOM declares
// NETN_Aggregate as PublishSubscribe, publishing Callsign, Symbol, Echelon,
// Status, UniqueID, EntityList, EmbeddedUnitList, HigherHeadquarters and
// Mounted. Every unit it registers — platoons, companies, battalions — is
// one of these. Dropping this class in the v4 migration is what stopped
// callsigns arriving; v4 moved the same attributes up onto AggregateEntity,
// so both are listed and whichever exists wins.
List<String> aggregateAttrs = List.of(
// EntityType and EntityIdentifier are declared on BaseEntity and were
// missing from this list, which is why aggregates arrived with a
// position and no DIS type: an inherited attribute still has to be
// named at the class the object is discovered at, and these were only
// named on the platform list.
"EntityType", "EntityIdentifier",
"Spatial", "AggregateMarking", "AggregateState", "ForceIdentifier",
"UniqueId", "UniqueID", "Callsign", "Symbol", "SymbolId", "Status",
"Echelon", "CombatValue", "SupportedActions",
"EntityList", "EmbeddedUnitList", "HigherHeadquarters", "Mounted",
"DisaggregatedEntities", "DividedEntities");
map.put("HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity.AggregateEntity", aggregateAttrs);
map.put("HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity.AggregateEntity.NETN_Aggregate", aggregateAttrs);
// The RTI's own view of who is joined. Not entities — reported separately.
map.put(Fom.MOM_FEDERATE, MOM_FEDERATE_ATTRIBUTES);
return Collections.unmodifiableMap(map);
}
// ── Config ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// The CRC (central RTI component) and the FedPro server are distinct
// endpoints and must not be assumed to share a host/port. This bridge only
// ever *talks* over FedPro (fedproAddress); the FedPro server reaches the CRC
// on our behalf, from inside the cluster.
//
// crcAddress is a comma-separated list of candidate CRC addresses, forwarded
// to the far side one at a time as LRC crcHost/crcPort settings until one
// connects — because the CRC's address is not stable here. With the Pitch
// booster enabled the CRC advertises the booster's host and port instead of
// its own, and connecting against the wrong one fails with "Failed to connect
// to CRC at ...", which is indistinguishable from the CRC being down. The
// first candidate is "default", meaning send nothing and let the FedPro
// server use its own configured address — the case that works when the CRC is
// directly reachable. See EnvironmentPorts.resolveCrcAddress().
private final String crcAddress;
private final String fedproAddress;
// FedPro connection settings, configurable via env vars so they can be
// tuned without a code change. Defaults are plain TCP with no SNI.
private final String fedproProtocol;
private final String fedproSniHostname;
/** Optional LRC advertised address; empty means let the LRC derive one. */
private final String lrcAdvertisedAddress;
private final int fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds;
private final String federationName;
private final String fomPath;
/**
* Path to the NETN MIM, or blank for the RTI's standard MIM.
*
* <p>Only usable when this bridge creates the federation: 1516-2010 accepts a
* MIM on {@code createFederationExecution} and nowhere else. It is the only
* declaration of {@code UniqueId}, so a federation created without it has no
* taskable entities — every NETN task names its Taskee by UUID.
*/
private final String mimPath;
private final boolean timeManagementEnabled;
private final EntityStateStore store;
/** Observational state for the /diagnostics endpoints. Never affects behaviour. */
private final BridgeDiagnostics diagnostics;
// ── Tasking dialects ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Both are constructed up front and one is selected per connection from the
// FOM actually loaded. They hold no connection state of their own, so a
// reconnect that lands on a federation running the other FOM version simply
// reselects.
// v2 removed: see selectDialect(). EtrV2Dialect still exists as a file but is
// no longer constructed, so nothing can select it.
private final TaskDialect v4Dialect;
/** "auto" (default), "v2" or "v4" — see BRIDGE_TASK_DIALECT. */
private final String dialectPreference;
/** The dialect in use, or null when this federation defines neither. */
private volatile TaskDialect dialect;
// ── HLA internals ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
private RTIambassador rtiAmbassador;
private FederationAmbassador federationAmbassador;
private final Map<String, InteractionClassHandle> interactionHandles = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private final Map<ObjectInstanceHandle, Entity> discoveredEntities =
Collections.synchronizedMap(new HashMap<>());
// ── Federate identity and role ────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// The federate name used to be the literal "CSE-HLA-Bridge". That is fine for
// exactly one bridge: an RTI keeps a name registered until the federate
// resigns or times out, so a second instance — a monitor-only bridge in
// another compute module, or the next pod after a hard restart that never got
// to resign — is refused with "Federate name CSE-HLA-Bridge already in use"
// and, the name being fixed, refused again on every retry. Configurable, with
// a suffix appended on collision, makes both cases work.
private final String preferredFederateName;
// The name actually joined under, which is not always the preferred one.
// Null until a join succeeds.
private volatile String federateName = null;
// The CRC candidate that last connected, tried first next time. Null until one
// succeeds, and cleared when every candidate fails — so the booster being
// switched on or off costs one slow connect rather than a permanent wrong
// guess. See buildConnectSettings() and EnvironmentPorts.resolveCrcAddress().
private volatile String workingCrcCandidate = null;
/**
* Monitor-only: subscribe to entity state, publish nothing, task nothing.
*
* <p>For the bridge instance that exists purely to feed the entity track
* stream. Beyond not advertising a capability it will never exercise, it also
* sidesteps the FOM merge failure — a monitor contributes no modules, so
* "Cannot merge BaseEntity - mismatch in attributes" cannot arise — and it
* needs no tasking dialect, so a federation that defines neither is not a
* problem for it.
*/
private final boolean monitorOnly;
/**
* Whether to create the federation execution when it does not exist.
*
* <p>Off by default. This bridge is a guest in a federation the simulators
* own, and when that execution is absent they are not running — creating an
* empty one in their place is how you get a bridge that reports
* {@code connected: true}, discovers nothing, receives nothing, and reads
* exactly like a subscription or FOM bug. It also seeds the execution with our
* v2-era {@code SWORD_FOM.xml}, after which a v4 simulator's join can be
* refused on a merge it would never otherwise have attempted.
*
* <p>False means an absent federation fails the attempt and
* {@link #connectWithRetry} keeps trying until the real one appears.
*/
private final boolean createFederationIfAbsent;
/**
* Whether this bridge created the execution it is currently joined to.
*
* <p>Reported by {@code /status} and {@code /diagnostics} because it is the
* difference between "I cannot see the entities" and "I am not where the
* entities are", and nothing else distinguishes the two from the outside.
*/
private volatile boolean federationCreatedByUs = false;
/**
* Whether to subscribe to the {@code HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity} catch-all.
*
* <p>Off by default, because measurement showed the catch-all was harmful. With
* it subscribed, this RTI reported {@code discoveredClass=HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity}
* for all 301 objects; with it removed, the same objects reported
* {@code …BaseEntity.AggregateEntity} — which is what SWORD's SOM declares it
* publishes. The RTI reports discovery at the catch-all whenever it is
* subscribed, not at the most specific subscribed class.
*
* <p>That governs attribute reachability, not just labelling:
* {@link FederationAmbassador#requestValuesForAllInstances} asks for the
* attributes registered against the <em>discovered</em> class, so a catch-all
* silently reduces every object to the eight BaseEntity attributes and nothing
* declared on PhysicalEntity or AggregateEntity is ever requested. The catch-all
* caused the missing entity fields it was added to prevent.
*/
private final boolean subscribeBaseEntity;
/**
* How often to re-ask publishers for current attribute values, in seconds; 0
* disables it. See the run loop and
* {@link FederationAmbassador#requestValuesForAllInstances()} — subscribing
* alone only entitles this federate to *future* updates, so a scenario that is
* paused, or loaded after we join, otherwise yields entities with names and no
* state.
*/
private final int attributeRefreshSeconds;
private final int attributeRefreshMaxRequests;
private volatile boolean running = false;
private volatile boolean reconnectRequested = false;
// Remembered from the initial connect so a reconnect can use the same policy.
private volatile int retryAttempts = 60;
private volatile int retryDelaySeconds = 10;
// ── Constructor (matches BridgeApplication call) ──────────────────────────
public BridgeFederate(String crcAddress,
String fedproAddress,
String fedproProtocol,
String fedproSniHostname,
String lrcAdvertisedAddress,
int fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds,
String federationName,
String fomPath,
String mimPath,
boolean timeManagementEnabled,
EntityStateStore store,
BridgeDiagnostics diagnostics,
TaskDialect.Identity taskIdentity,
String dialectPreference,
String federateName,
String mode,
boolean createFederationIfAbsent,
boolean subscribeBaseEntity,
int attributeRefreshSeconds,
int attributeRefreshMaxRequests) {
this.preferredFederateName = federateName == null || federateName.isBlank()
? "CSE-HLA-Bridge" : federateName.trim();
this.monitorOnly = "monitor".equalsIgnoreCase(mode == null ? "" : mode.trim());
this.createFederationIfAbsent = createFederationIfAbsent;
this.subscribeBaseEntity = subscribeBaseEntity;
this.attributeRefreshSeconds = attributeRefreshSeconds;
this.attributeRefreshMaxRequests = attributeRefreshMaxRequests;
this.diagnostics                 = diagnostics;
this.crcAddress                  = crcAddress;
this.fedproAddress               = fedproAddress;
this.fedproProtocol              = fedproProtocol;
this.fedproSniHostname           = fedproSniHostname;
this.lrcAdvertisedAddress        = lrcAdvertisedAddress;
this.fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds = fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds;
this.federationName              = federationName;
this.fomPath                     = fomPath;
this.mimPath                     = mimPath;
this.timeManagementEnabled       = timeManagementEnabled;
this.store                       = store;
this.dialectPreference           = dialectPreference == null || dialectPreference.isBlank()
? "auto" : dialectPreference.trim().toLowerCase();
this.v4Dialect                   = new SmcV4Dialect(this, taskIdentity);
}
/**
* The federation execution does not exist yet.
*
* <p>Separated from every other connect failure because it is not a fault of
* this bridge's: before an exercise starts, the simulators simply have not
* created their federation. So it is waited on indefinitely rather than
* counted against the retry budget — a bounded wait would have the bridge
* exhaust {@code BRIDGE_RETRY_ATTEMPTS} in ten minutes and then sit there
* alive and permanently disconnected until someone restarted the pod.
*/
public static class FederationNotRunningException extends IllegalStateException {
FederationNotRunningException(String message, Throwable cause) {
super(message, cause);
}
}
// ── Connection with retry ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
public void connectWithRetry(int maxAttempts, int delaySeconds) throws Exception {
this.retryAttempts     = maxAttempts;
this.retryDelaySeconds = delaySeconds;
Exception lastException = null;
// Counted manually rather than in a for-header: waiting for a federation
// that does not exist yet must not consume an attempt (see below).
int attempt = 0;
while (attempt < maxAttempts) {
try {
logger.info("Connection attempt {}/{} to federation '{}'",
attempt + 1, maxAttempts, federationName);
connect();
logger.info("Successfully connected to federation '{}'", federationName);
store.setConnected(true);
store.setFederationName(federationName);
diagnostics.recordConnected();
return;
} catch (FederationNotRunningException waiting) {
// Normal before an exercise starts, so INFO rather than WARN, and
// deliberately not an attempt: this loop should still be asking
// hours later, because the useful moment is whenever the
// simulators do come up.
lastException = waiting;
diagnostics.recordConnectionError(waiting.getMessage());
logger.info("Federation '{}' is not running yet — waiting {}s and asking again "
+ "(this does not count against the {} retry attempts). {}",
federationName, delaySeconds, maxAttempts, waiting.getMessage());
abandonFailedAttempt();
sleepBetweenAttempts(delaySeconds);
} catch (Exception e) {
attempt++;
lastException = e;
// Keep the reason: /diagnostics can then explain *why* the bridge
// is not connected instead of only that it is not.
diagnostics.recordConnectionError(e.getMessage());
logger.warn("Attempt {}/{} failed: {}. Retrying in {}s...",
attempt, maxAttempts, e.getMessage(), delaySeconds);
abandonFailedAttempt();
if (attempt < maxAttempts) {
sleepBetweenAttempts(delaySeconds);
}
}
}
throw new RuntimeException("Failed to connect after " + maxAttempts +
" attempts", lastException);
}
private static void sleepBetweenAttempts(int delaySeconds) throws InterruptedException {
try {
Thread.sleep(delaySeconds * 1000L);
} catch (InterruptedException ie) {
Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
throw ie;
}
}
/**
* Tear down a half-established connection after a failed attempt.
*
* <p>Without this, every retry leaks a FedPro session. The client keeps the
* session open — visible as continuing {@code CTRL_HEARTBEAT} traffic from
* every previous attempt — and the FedPro server allocates a fresh LRC port
* for each one, so the advertised address climbs {@code 6101, 6102,
* 6103 …} with a dead session left behind per attempt. With the default 60
* attempts that is 60 abandoned sessions on a server shared with the other
* simulators, which presents as a Pitch-side fault while actually being
* ours.
*
* <p>A failure can occur before or after joining the federation, so the
* resign and the disconnect are attempted separately and both are
* best-effort: there may be nothing to resign from, and the transport may
* already be gone.
*/
private void abandonFailedAttempt() {
RTIambassador ambassador = rtiAmbassador;
if (ambassador == null) {
return;
}
try {
ambassador.resignFederationExecution(ResignAction.DELETE_OBJECTS_THEN_DIVEST);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("Nothing to resign after the failed attempt: {}", e.getMessage());
}
try {
ambassador.disconnect();
logger.debug("Closed the FedPro session left by the failed attempt.");
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("Could not close the FedPro session cleanly: {}", e.getMessage());
} finally {
rtiAmbassador = null;
federationAmbassador = null;
interactionHandles.clear();
}
}
/**
* The local-settings-designator string for one CRC candidate.
*
* <p>{@code crcAddress} is the FedPro client's one special case: per its
* doc/Settings.md and {@code RTIambassadorClientGenericBase
* .extractAndRemoveClientSettings()}, it is rewritten into FedPro
* connect.hostname/connect.port and removed from the list — so it names the
* FedPro server, not the CRC.
*
* <p>Everything else that is not prefixed with {@code FedPro.} is forwarded
* to the RTI as a plain LRC setting, which is how {@code crcHost}/{@code
* crcPort} reach the far side and tell *it* where the CRC is. An earlier
* version sent those two set to the FedPro server's own address, which is
* why they appeared to do nothing.
*/
private String buildConnectSettings(String crcCandidate) {
String settings = "crcAddress=" + fedproAddress;
if (!EnvironmentPorts.DEFAULT_CRC.equals(crcCandidate)) {
int separator = crcCandidate.lastIndexOf(':');
String host = separator > 0 ? crcCandidate.substring(0, separator) : crcCandidate;
String port = separator > 0 ? crcCandidate.substring(separator + 1) : "8989";
settings += "\ncrcHost=" + host + "\ncrcPort=" + port;
}
if (lrcAdvertisedAddress != null && !lrcAdvertisedAddress.isBlank()) {
// The address the CRC is told to reach this LRC on. Left unset, the
// LRC derives one — on this deployment a cluster-internal DNS name
// plus an allocated port — and a join is refused outright if the CRC
// cannot verify it. Pinning something the CRC can definitely reach,
// such as this pod's own address, makes the join deterministic
// instead of dependent on which port the FedPro server handed out.
settings += "\naddress=" + lrcAdvertisedAddress;
}
return settings;
}
// ── Core connect logic ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
private void connect() throws Exception {
// FedPro settings are resolved with the following priority (highest
// to lowest): system property "FedProOverrides.*" > env var
// FEDPRO_CLIENT_OVERRIDES > RtiConfiguration/connect() parameter >
// system property "FedPro.*" > env var FEDPRO_CLIENT_SETTINGS >
// built-in defaults (connect.hostname=localhost, connect.port=15164).
// Set the "FedPro.connect.*" system properties before creating the
// RTI Ambassador so the factory picks them up at init.
String[] fedProParts = fedproAddress.split(":");
String fedProHost = fedProParts[0];
String fedProPort = fedProParts.length > 1 ? fedProParts[1] : "15164";
System.setProperty("FedPro.connect.hostname", fedProHost);
System.setProperty("FedPro.connect.port", fedProPort);
System.setProperty("FedPro.connect.protocol", fedproProtocol);
System.setProperty("FedPro.connect.timeout", String.valueOf(fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds));
if (fedproSniHostname != null && !fedproSniHostname.isBlank()) {
System.setProperty("FedPro.tls.sniHostname", fedproSniHostname);
}
logger.info(
"Set FedPro.connect.hostname={} FedPro.connect.port={} FedPro.connect.protocol={} FedPro.connect.timeout={}s FedPro.tls.sniHostname={}",
fedProHost, fedProPort, fedproProtocol, fedproConnectTimeoutSeconds,
(fedproSniHostname == null || fedproSniHostname.isBlank()) ? "(unset)" : fedproSniHostname);
// The CRC address is not stable on this deployment — with the Pitch
// booster enabled the CRC advertises a different host and port — so try
// each candidate in turn rather than assuming one. A candidate that has
// already worked is tried first on a reconnect, so the common case costs
// a single attempt.
List<String> candidates = EnvironmentPorts.splitCandidates(crcAddress);
String known = workingCrcCandidate;
if (known != null && candidates.remove(known)) {
candidates.add(0, known);
}
logger.info("CRC candidates to try, in order: {}", candidates);
Exception lastFailure = null;
for (String candidate : candidates) {
// A fresh ambassador per candidate: a failed connect can leave a
// half-open FedPro session on the old one, and reusing it produces
// "Already has a session" instead of the real error — which is
// exactly what the previous no-settings fallback did on every
// failure, making one CRC outage look like two distinct faults.
rtiAmbassador = RtiFactoryFactory.getRtiFactory().getRtiAmbassador();
federationAmbassador =
new FederationAmbassador(rtiAmbassador, store, diagnostics, this::onConnectionLost);
String settings = buildConnectSettings(candidate);
logger.info("Connecting via CRC candidate '{}' with settings: {}",
candidate, settings.replace("\n", " | "));
try {
rtiAmbassador.connect(federationAmbassador, CallbackModel.HLA_IMMEDIATE, settings);
workingCrcCandidate = candidate;
lastFailure = null;
logger.info("Connected to RTI via FedPro {} using CRC candidate '{}'.",
fedproAddress, candidate);
break;
} catch (Exception e) {
lastFailure = e;
logger.info("CRC candidate '{}' did not connect: {}", candidate, e.getMessage());
// Close the session this attempt may have opened before trying
// the next candidate, or the FedPro server accumulates one
// abandoned session (and one advertised LRC port) per candidate.
abandonFailedAttempt();
}
}
if (lastFailure != null) {
workingCrcCandidate = null;
throw lastFailure;
}
// Step 3: Join the federation, creating it only if explicitly allowed.
//
// Creating it used to be unconditional, which meant an absent federation —
// i.e. the simulators not running — was silently replaced by an empty
// execution of our own. The bridge then reports connected=true with
// entities 0 and interactions 0, which is indistinguishable from a
// subscription gap and sends the next person to read the FOM. Waiting is
// the useful behaviour; standing in for the simulators is not.
// See BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION.
List<URL> fomUrls = resolveFomUrls(fomPath);
federationCreatedByUs = false;
if (createFederationIfAbsent) {
try {
// With a MIM configured, use the overload that takes one. The NETN
// MIM extends HLAobjectRoot with UniqueId, which the merged FOM
// relies on and the RTI's standard MIM does not provide — create
// without it and every entity is untaskable for want of a UUID.
URL mimUrl = resolveMimUrl(mimPath);
if (mimUrl == null) {
logger.warn("Creating federation '{}' with the RTI's standard MIM: MIM_PATH is "
+ "not set. UniqueId is declared by the NETN MIM, so entities in this "
+ "federation will have no UUID and cannot be tasked.", federationName);
rtiAmbassador.createFederationExecution(
federationName,
fomUrls.toArray(new URL[0]));
} else {
logger.info("Creating federation '{}' with the NETN MIM at {}.",
federationName, mimUrl);
rtiAmbassador.createFederationExecution(
federationName,
fomUrls.toArray(new URL[0]),
mimUrl);
}
federationCreatedByUs = true;
logger.warn("Federation '{}' did not exist and was CREATED by this bridge "
+ "(BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION=true). Every other federate is therefore "
+ "somewhere else and no entities will ever be discovered here. If the "
+ "simulators are expected to already be running, check FEDERATION_NAME "
+ "and CSE_ENVIRONMENT against theirs, and set BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION"
+ "=false so the bridge waits for their federation instead.",
federationName);
} catch (FederationExecutionAlreadyExists e) {
logger.info("Federation '{}' already exists — joining.", federationName);
}
}
try {
joinFederation(fomUrls);
} catch (FederationExecutionDoesNotExist e) {
// Deliberately a failed attempt rather than a created federation: the
// retry loop will keep asking, and the next attempt succeeds the
// moment the simulators bring their own execution up.
throw new FederationNotRunningException(
"Federation '" + federationName + "' does not exist on the CRC reached via "
+ "candidate '" + workingCrcCandidate + "'. The simulators are either not "
+ "running or are on a different CRC — compare with a bridge that does see "
+ "entities. Retrying rather than creating an empty federation; set "
+ "BRIDGE_CREATE_FEDERATION=true if this bridge is meant to create it.", e);
}
// Identity of this connection, for /status and /diagnostics. Two bridges
// with identical configuration can land in different places — the CRC
// address is not stable here, so each instance learns its own working
// candidate — and until this was recorded, nothing in the API said which.
diagnostics.recordIdentity(federateName, workingCrcCandidate, federationCreatedByUs);
// Step 4: Handles, pub/sub, time
initializeHandles();
publishAndSubscribe();
enableTimeManagement();
}
// ── Run loop (blocks until disconnected) ──────────────────────────────────
public void run() {
running = true;
logger.info("HLA bridge run loop started.");
long lastAttributeRefresh = System.currentTimeMillis();
while (running) {
try {
// With HLA_IMMEDIATE callbacks are delivered automatically on
// RTI-managed threads, so this thread only has to stay alive
// and handle reconnection.
Thread.sleep(1000);
} catch (InterruptedException e) {
Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
break;
}
if (reconnectRequested && running) {
reconnectRequested = false;
reconnect();
lastAttributeRefresh = System.currentTimeMillis();
continue;
}
// Re-ask for current attribute values periodically. One request at
// discovery is not always answered — the publisher may not be ready,
// the scenario may be loaded after we join, or a paused federation may
// simply have nothing to send yet. Without this, an entity discovered
// during a pause stays a name with no position indefinitely.
if (attributeRefreshSeconds > 0
&& federationAmbassador != null
&& System.currentTimeMillis() - lastAttributeRefresh
>= attributeRefreshSeconds * 1000L) {
lastAttributeRefresh = System.currentTimeMillis();
try {
FederationAmbassador.RefreshSummary summary =
federationAmbassador.requestValuesForAllInstances(attributeRefreshMaxRequests);
if (summary.asked() > 0) {
logger.debug("Requested current attribute values for {} of {} incomplete "
+ "instance(s); {} already complete.{}",
summary.asked(), summary.outstanding(), summary.complete(),
summary.capped()
? " Capped by BRIDGE_ATTRIBUTE_REFRESH_MAX_REQUESTS; the"
+ " remainder are asked on the next cycle."
: "");
}
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("Attribute value refresh failed: {}", e.getMessage());
}
}
}
logger.info("HLA bridge run loop ended.");
}
/**
* Called by {@link FederationAmbassador#connectionLost} on an RTI callback
* thread. Only sets a flag: the actual reconnect happens on the run loop
* thread, since re-entering the RTI from inside a callback is unsafe.
*/
void onConnectionLost() {
reconnectRequested = true;
}
private void reconnect() {
logger.warn("Re-establishing the RTI connection after connection loss...");
// The old ambassador and every handle resolved through it are dead.
rtiAmbassador = null;
federationAmbassador = null;
interactionHandles.clear();
// The dialect was a property of the FOM we were connected to. Reselected
// on reconnect, since a restarted federation can come back with a
// different FOM.
dialect = null;
// Described the execution we just lost, not the one we are about to join.
federationCreatedByUs = false;
// Entity state came from a federation we are no longer attached to;
// keeping it would serve stale positions over the REST API.
store.clear();
store.setConnected(false);
// FOM classes, federates and traffic counts described the federation we
// just lost. Task history is kept — what was attempted before a
// reconnect is exactly what a debugging session wants.
diagnostics.clearConnectionState();
try {
connectWithRetry(retryAttempts, retryDelaySeconds);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.error("Could not re-establish the RTI connection: {}", e.getMessage(), e);
}
}
public void stop() {
running = false;
}
/**
* Join the federation, preferring to contribute our own FOM modules but
* falling back to the federation's existing FOM if they cannot be merged.
*
* <p>Supplying modules on join adds any classes the federation lacks, which
* is what we want when the federation was created by a federate with a
* narrower FOM. But HLA only merges modules that define shared classes
* <em>identically</em>. Joining a federation created by another federate
* whose FOM defines, say, {@code BaseEntity} with a different attribute set
* fails outright:
*
* <pre>
* Error merging FOM module file:/tmp/SWORD_FOM….xml
* Cannot merge BaseEntity - mismatch in attributes.
* </pre>
*
* <p>That is a worse outcome than not contributing modules at all: without
* them we still see everything the federation defines, whereas a failed
* join sees nothing. So try with, then without.
*
* <p>Joining without our modules means classes the federation does not
* define are simply unavailable — {@link #initializeHandles()} already logs
* those and the endpoints that need them fail individually.
*/
private void joinFederation(List<URL> fomUrls) throws Exception {
// One attempt. connectWithRetry already retries the whole connect-and-join
// cycle, and an inner retry loop only delayed the real error: the failures
// seen on this deployment — a FOM that cannot merge, and an advertised
// address other federates cannot reach — are not transient, so trying
// again in fifteen seconds achieves nothing except hiding them for longer.
joinOnce(fomUrls);
}
/**
* Join under the configured name, or under a suffixed one if it is taken.
*
* <p>A name collision is not transient and not a FOM problem: waiting does not
* clear it while the holder is alive, and the RTI keeps an unresigned name
* registered for FED_TIMEOUT_RECONNECT — ten minutes here — after a hard
* restart. Either way the fix is the same, and renaming costs nothing: no
* other component identifies this federate by name.
*/
private void joinOnce(List<URL> fomUrls) throws Exception {
try {
attemptJoin(preferredFederateName, fomUrls);
return;
} catch (Exception first) {
if (!isNameAlreadyInUse(first)) {
throw first;
}
String unique = preferredFederateName + "-" + instanceSuffix();
