logger.warn("Federate name '{}' is already registered in '{}' — either another bridge "
+ "is live or a previous run did not resign and the RTI has not expired it "
+ "yet. Joining as '{}' instead of waiting it out.",
preferredFederateName, federationName, unique);
attemptJoin(unique, fomUrls);
}
}
/**
* Whether an exception means the federate name is taken.
*
* <p>Matched on the message as well as the type: FedPro tunnels the far side's
* exception, and a wrapped one arrives as a generic RTI failure carrying the
* original text.
*/
private static boolean isNameAlreadyInUse(Exception e) {
if (e instanceof FederateNameAlreadyInUse) {
return true;
}
String message = e.getMessage();
return message != null && message.toLowerCase().contains("already in use");
}
/**
* A short suffix identifying this instance, so two live federates are
* distinguishable in the RTI's federate list rather than merely unique. The
* pod name is the most useful thing available; a clock-derived value is the
* fallback outside Kubernetes.
*/
private static String instanceSuffix() {
String hostname = System.getenv("HOSTNAME");
if (hostname != null && !hostname.isBlank()) {
String cleaned = hostname.replaceAll("[^A-Za-z0-9]", "");
if (!cleaned.isEmpty()) {
return cleaned.length() > 6 ? cleaned.substring(cleaned.length() - 6) : cleaned;
}
}
return Long.toHexString(System.nanoTime() & 0xFFFFFL);
}
private void attemptJoin(String name, List<URL> fomUrls) throws Exception {
// A monitor contributes nothing, so there is nothing to merge and no
// reason to risk the merge failing. Straight to the federation's own FOM.
if (monitorOnly) {
rtiAmbassador.joinFederationExecution(name, "BridgeFederateType", federationName);
federateName = name;
logger.info("Joined federation '{}' as '{}' in monitor-only mode, using its "
+ "existing FOM.", federationName, name);
return;
}
try {
rtiAmbassador.joinFederationExecution(
name, "BridgeFederateType", federationName,
fomUrls.toArray(new URL[0]));
federateName = name;
logger.info("Joined federation '{}' as '{}', contributing our FOM modules.",
federationName, name);
return;
} catch (Exception withModules) {
// A taken name will be taken again without our modules, so let the
// caller rename rather than logging a misleading FOM warning first.
if (isNameAlreadyInUse(withModules)) {
throw withModules;
}
logger.warn("Could not join '{}' while contributing our FOM modules ({}). "
+ "Retrying with the federation's existing FOM — any class it does not "
+ "define will be unavailable, and the endpoints needing it will fail.",
federationName, withModules.getMessage());
try {
rtiAmbassador.joinFederationExecution(
name, "BridgeFederateType", federationName);
federateName = name;
logger.info("Joined federation '{}' as '{}' using its existing FOM.",
federationName, name);
return;
} catch (Exception withoutModules) {
if (isNameAlreadyInUse(withoutModules)) {
throw withoutModules;
}
// Report the fallback's failure, not the first one. Contributing
// our modules is optional and its failure is often expected —
// SWORD_FOM.xml is v2-era and cannot merge with a v4 federation's
// BaseEntity, so "Cannot merge BaseEntity" is noise here. The
// fallback failing is what actually stops the join, and burying it
// at DEBUG while shouting the merge error sent us reading the FOM
// when the real problem was that no one can reach the address we
// advertise.
logger.warn("Join failed both ways. Contributing our FOM modules: {}. "
+ "Using the federation's own FOM: {}",
withModules.getMessage(), withoutModules.getMessage());
throw withoutModules;
}
}
}
/**
* The configured MIM as a URL, or null when none is set.
*
* <p>A missing file is reported as null rather than thrown: a MIM only applies
* when creating the federation, and failing the whole connect over it would
* turn a create-path misconfiguration into a bridge that cannot even monitor.
*/
private URL resolveMimUrl(String path) {
if (path == null || path.isBlank()) {
return null;
}
File mim = new File(path.trim());
if (!mim.isFile()) {
logger.warn("MIM_PATH is set to '{}' but there is no file there — creating with the "
+ "RTI's standard MIM instead.", path);
return null;
}
try {
return mim.toURI().toURL();
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.warn("Could not turn MIM path '{}' into a URL: {}", path, e.getMessage());
return null;
}
}
// ── FOM URL resolution ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
private List<URL> resolveFomUrls(String fomPath) throws Exception {
List<URL> urls = new ArrayList<>();
File fomDir = new File(fomPath);
if (fomDir.isDirectory()) {
File[] fomFiles = fomDir.listFiles(
(dir, name) -> name.endsWith(".xml") || name.endsWith(".fed"));
if (fomFiles != null) {
for (File f : fomFiles) {
urls.add(f.toURI().toURL());
logger.info("FOM file: {}", f.getName());
}
}
} else {
// Treat as single file path
urls.add(new File(fomPath).toURI().toURL());
}
if (urls.isEmpty()) {
throw new IllegalStateException("No FOM files found at: " + fomPath);
}
return urls;
}
// ── Handle initialisation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
private void initializeHandles() throws Exception {
logger.info("Resolving FOM interaction handles...");
List<String> missing = new ArrayList<>();
for (Map.Entry<String, String> known : allKnownInteractions().entrySet()) {
String name = known.getKey();
try {
InteractionClassHandle handle = rtiAmbassador.getInteractionClassHandle(name);
interactionHandles.put(name, handle);
federationAmbassador.registerInteractionName(handle, name);
diagnostics.recordFomClass(name, known.getValue(), true);
} catch (NameNotFound e) {
diagnostics.recordFomClass(name, known.getValue(), false);
// Don't abort the whole connection for one optional class: an
// absent class only breaks the endpoints that use it, and the
// FOM in use may legitimately omit some modules. With two
// dialects known, roughly half of these are *expected* to be
// missing — whichever dialect this federation does not speak.
missing.add(name);
}
}
logger.info("Resolved {} of {} known interaction classes.",
interactionHandles.size(), interactionHandles.size() + missing.size());
if (!missing.isEmpty()) {
logger.debug("Not present in the loaded FOM: {}", missing);
}
selectDialect();
}
/**
* Every interaction class name either dialect might use, plus the
* dialect-independent RPR ones.
*
* <p>All of them are resolved regardless of which dialect ends up selected:
* the resolution result is the evidence for the selection, and it is also
* what makes "this federation has NETN-SE but not NETN-LOG" visible in the
* log instead of being discovered later by a failing endpoint.
*/
/**
* v2-era class names, resolved and reported but never published or subscribed.
*
* <p>Kept purely so {@code /diagnostics/fom} can still answer "which
* generations does this federation define?". Removing v2 from the dialects also
* removed these from the enumeration, and with them the ability to see that a
* federation was running the other generation — which is the single question
* that cost the most time to answer. Reporting a class is free; publishing or
* subscribing to it is what would resurrect v2 tasking, and neither happens
* here.
*/
private static final List<String> V2_REPORTED_ONLY = List.of(
Fom.MOVE_TO_LOCATION, Fom.MOVE_TO_ENTITY, Fom.PATROL, Fom.MAGIC_MOVE,
Fom.FIRE_AT_LOCATION, Fom.FIRE_AT_ENTITY, Fom.SET_RULES_OF_ENGAGEMENT,
Fom.CREATE_OBSTACLE, Fom.DISRUPT_COMMUNICATION, Fom.STOP_AT_SIDE_OF_ROAD,
Fom.TURN_TO_HEADING, Fom.SET_ORDERED_SPEED, Fom.SET_ORDERED_ALTITUDE,
Fom.CANCEL_ALL_TASKS, Fom.TASK_STATUS_REPORT,
Fom.MRM_AGGREGATE, Fom.MRM_DISAGGREGATE, Fom.MRM_RESPONSE);
private Map<String, String> allKnownInteractions() {
// Name to dialect tag, so the diagnostics endpoints can group by dialect
// and by FOM module. LinkedHashMap keeps resolution order stable in logs.
Map<String, String> all = new LinkedHashMap<>();
for (String name : RPR_PUBLISHED)  all.putIfAbsent(name, "rpr");
for (String name : RPR_SUBSCRIBED) all.putIfAbsent(name, "rpr");
for (TaskDialect candidate : List.of(v4Dialect)) {
for (String name : candidate.published())  all.putIfAbsent(name, candidate.id());
for (String name : candidate.subscribed()) all.putIfAbsent(name, candidate.id());
for (String name : candidate.optional())   all.putIfAbsent(name, candidate.id());
}
// Reported, never used — see V2_REPORTED_ONLY.
for (String name : V2_REPORTED_ONLY) all.putIfAbsent(name, "v2");
return all;
}
/**
* Decide which tasking dialect this federation speaks.
*
* <p>The bridge may join a federation created by another federate, so the
* dialect is not ours to choose. Getting it wrong is not an error the RTI
* reports: the class is simply absent (a 409 from the REST layer) or, worse,
* present with different parameters. Deciding from the loaded FOM and saying
* so once at startup turns "why did nothing happen?" into a log line.
*
* <p>{@code BRIDGE_TASK_DIALECT=v2|v4} forces a choice, for the case where a
* federation defines both trees and the simulators only act on one. A forced
* dialect that the FOM does not define is refused rather than used, because
* every task would 409 with a confusing message.
*/
private void selectDialect() {
boolean v4Present = hasInteraction(v4Dialect.detectionClass());
logger.info("FOM tasking dialect: v4={} ({})", v4Present, v4Dialect.detectionClass());
// v4 only. The v2-era ETR_Root.ETR_Task tree belongs to a different
// generation of the model and is no longer wired up: CSE-Prep runs the
// merged NETN/RPR FOM, and supporting both meant a federation carrying the
// wrong generation silently selected it — every task then went out in a
// format nothing was listening for, with a 200 from the REST layer either
// way. EtrV2Dialect is left in the tree for reference and for
// HlaEncoderTest's wire vectors; nothing constructs it.
if ("v2".equals(dialectPreference)) {
logger.error("BRIDGE_TASK_DIALECT=v2 was requested but v2 support has been removed. "
+ "Ignoring the preference and using v4 if this FOM defines it.");
}
TaskDialect selected = v4Present ? v4Dialect : null;
if (selected == null && !"auto".equals(dialectPreference)) {
logger.error("BRIDGE_TASK_DIALECT={} was requested but this federation's FOM does not "
+ "define it. Tasking is disabled rather than sending classes the federation "
+ "cannot receive. Unset BRIDGE_TASK_DIALECT to select automatically.",
dialectPreference);
}
dialect = selected;
diagnostics.recordDialect(
selected == null ? "none" : selected.id(),
selected == null ? "none" : selected.name(),
dialectPreference);
if (selected == null) {
logger.warn("No NETN tasking dialect available in this federation's FOM. Entities can "
+ "be monitored but not tasked; every tasking endpoint will return 409.");
} else {
logger.info("Tasking dialect selected: {} (preference '{}'). Publishing {} class(es).",
selected.name(), dialectPreference, selected.published().size());
logReportingCapability(selected);
}
}
/**
* Log which of the selected dialect's report classes exist.
*
* <p>Task acknowledgement is the difference between "the task was sent" and
* "the task was accepted": without the status/response class, a task that no
* federate acts on is indistinguishable from one that succeeded.
*/
private void logReportingCapability(TaskDialect selected) {
List<String> availableReports = new ArrayList<>();
List<String> missingReports = new ArrayList<>();
for (String name : selected.subscribed()) {
(hasInteraction(name) ? availableReports : missingReports).add(shortName(name));
}
logger.info("Task feedback and reports available: {}", availableReports);
if (!missingReports.isEmpty()) {
logger.warn("Not available in this FOM, so the corresponding feedback will never "
+ "arrive: {}", missingReports);
}
}
private static String shortName(String fomClassName) {
int lastDot = fomClassName.lastIndexOf('.');
return lastDot < 0 ? fomClassName : fomClassName.substring(lastDot + 1);
}
// ── Publish / Subscribe ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
private void publishAndSubscribe() throws Exception {
logger.info("Setting up pub/sub...");
for (Map.Entry<String, List<String>> entry : SUBSCRIBED_OBJECTS.entrySet()) {
if ("HLAobjectRoot.BaseEntity".equals(entry.getKey())) {
if (!subscribeBaseEntity) {
logger.info("Not subscribing to the {} catch-all (the default). This RTI "
+ "reports discovery at the catch-all whenever it is subscribed, which "
+ "would limit every object to BaseEntity attributes. An object "
+ "published as a class this bridge does not enumerate is invisible "
+ "rather than appearing as a bare BaseEntity — see /diagnostics/objects.",
entry.getKey());
continue;
}
logger.warn("Subscribing to the {} catch-all (BRIDGE_SUBSCRIBE_BASE_ENTITY=true). "
+ "Measured on this RTI: with this subscribed, EVERY object is discovered "
+ "at BaseEntity, so only its eight attributes are ever requested and "
+ "ForceIdentifier/Marking/DamageState/Echelon/CombatValue/AggregateState "
+ "will stay null. Use it only to identify an object published as an "
+ "unenumerated class, then turn it back off.", entry.getKey());
}
subscribeObjectClass(entry.getKey(), entry.getValue());
}
// A monitor publishes nothing at all: it exists to read entity state for
// the track stream, and advertising tasking classes it will never send is
// exactly the misleading-healthy-startup problem described below.
//
// The report subscriptions and the MOM federate query further down still
// run in monitor mode. Listening is free, none of it feeds the entity
// state the stream carries — that arrives as object attribute
// reflections, not interactions — and the MOM federate list is precisely
// how you find out which federate is holding a name you wanted.
if (monitorOnly) {
logger.info("Monitor-only mode: subscribing to entity state and reports, publishing "
+ "no interaction classes. Tasking endpoints on this instance return 409.");
} else {
// Publish only what the selected dialect actually sends. Publishing the
// other dialect's classes would advertise a capability this bridge will
// never exercise, and publishing classes we cannot send is what made the
// previous startup log look healthy while every task 409'd.
List<String> toPublish = new ArrayList<>(RPR_PUBLISHED);
if (dialect != null) {
toPublish.addAll(dialect.published());
}
for (String name : toPublish) {
InteractionClassHandle handle = interactionHandles.get(name);
if (handle == null) continue;
try {
rtiAmbassador.publishInteractionClass(handle);
diagnostics.recordPublished(name);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.warn("Could not publish {}: {}", name, e.getMessage());
}
}
}
// Subscribe to both dialects' reports regardless of the selection.
// Listening costs nothing, absent classes are skipped, and a federation
// that sends v4 reports while we task in v2 (or the reverse) is exactly
// the situation where seeing the traffic matters most.
List<String> toSubscribe = new ArrayList<>(RPR_SUBSCRIBED);
toSubscribe.addAll(v4Dialect.subscribed());
for (String name : new java.util.LinkedHashSet<>(toSubscribe)) {
InteractionClassHandle handle = interactionHandles.get(name);
if (handle == null) continue;
try {
rtiAmbassador.subscribeInteractionClass(handle);
diagnostics.recordSubscribed(name);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.warn("Could not subscribe {}: {}", name, e.getMessage());
}
}
requestMomFederateUpdates();
logger.info("Pub/sub configured.");
}
/**
* Ask the RTI to report the MOM federate attributes now.
*
* <p>MOM instances are discovered as federates join, but the RTI only sends
* their attribute values on request (or on a reporting period). Without this
* we would learn how many federates are present but not who they are.
*/
/**
* Ask the RTI again for MOM federate details.
*
* <p>Exposed for {@code POST /diagnostics/refresh-federates}: the automatic
* request happens once at connect, so a federate that joined later — the
* usual case when a simulator is started after the bridge — is missing from
* the reported list until asked for again.
*/
public void refreshFederateReports() {
requestMomFederateUpdates();
}
private void requestMomFederateUpdates() {
try {
ObjectClassHandle momFederate = rtiAmbassador.getObjectClassHandle(Fom.MOM_FEDERATE);
AttributeHandleSet attributes = rtiAmbassador.getAttributeHandleSetFactory().create();
for (String attributeName : MOM_FEDERATE_ATTRIBUTES) {
try {
attributes.add(rtiAmbassador.getAttributeHandle(momFederate, attributeName));
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("MOM attribute {} not in FOM, skipping.", attributeName);
}
}
if (attributes.isEmpty()) return;
rtiAmbassador.requestAttributeValueUpdate(momFederate, attributes, null);
logger.info("Requested MOM federate details — each federate joined to '{}' "
+ "should be reported below.", federationName);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("Could not request MOM federate updates: {}", e.getMessage());
}
}
/**
* Subscribe to an object class, skipping the class or any individual
* attribute that the loaded FOM does not define. Different simulators
* publish different NETN subclasses, so a missing class is normal and must
* not abort the connection.
*/
private void subscribeObjectClass(String className, List<String> attributeNames) {
ObjectClassHandle classHandle;
try {
classHandle = rtiAmbassador.getObjectClassHandle(className);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.debug("Object class not in FOM, skipping: {}", className);
diagnostics.recordObjectClass(className, false, List.of(), attributeNames);
return;
}
try {
AttributeHandleSet attributes = rtiAmbassador.getAttributeHandleSetFactory().create();
// Tracked by name so /diagnostics/objects can report which attributes
// this FOM actually defined. An attribute skipped here is the difference
// between an entity with an identity and one with only a name, and it
// used to be visible solely as a DEBUG log line.
List<String> gotAttributes = new ArrayList<>();
List<String> missingAttributes = new ArrayList<>();
for (String attributeName : attributeNames) {
try {
AttributeHandle attributeHandle =
rtiAmbassador.getAttributeHandle(classHandle, attributeName);
attributes.add(attributeHandle);
gotAttributes.add(attributeName);
// Register so FederationAmbassador can decode by name.
federationAmbassador.registerAttributeName(attributeHandle, attributeName);
} catch (Exception e) {
missingAttributes.add(attributeName);
logger.debug("Attribute {}.{} not in FOM, skipping.", className, attributeName);
}
}
if (attributes.isEmpty()) {
// The class exists but defines none of the attributes we want, so we
// do not subscribe — and objects published as it are then discovered
// at whatever ancestor we did subscribe to, losing everything below.
logger.info("Object class {} defines none of the {} attributes wanted — not "
+ "subscribed. Entities published as it will be discovered at an "
+ "ancestor class instead.", className, attributeNames.size());
diagnostics.recordObjectClass(className, false, List.of(), missingAttributes);
return;
}
// Register BEFORE subscribing, not after.
//
// Callbacks are HLA_IMMEDIATE, so the RTI delivers discoverObjectInstance
// on its own threads the moment a subscription lands — and joining a
// federation that already holds hundreds of objects produces that burst
// instantly. Registering afterwards loses the race for every entity in
// it, twice over:
//
//   * classNames has no entry yet, so the entity is classified "unknown"
//     however specific its class actually was;
//   * subscribedAttributes has no entry yet, so requestCurrentValues()
//     looks up null and asks for nothing at all — leaving a name with no
//     position, no type and no identity until the periodic refresh comes
//     round, or permanently if BRIDGE_ATTRIBUTE_REFRESH_SECONDS is 0.
//
// Registering first is safe: these maps only describe what we intend to
// subscribe to, and an entry for a subscription that then fails is inert,
// because nothing will ever be discovered against it.
federationAmbassador.registerObjectClassName(classHandle, className);
// Remembered so the ambassador can ask for current values on discovery
// and again periodically — subscribing alone only entitles us to future
// updates, which a paused publisher never sends.
federationAmbassador.registerSubscribedAttributes(classHandle, attributes);
rtiAmbassador.subscribeObjectClassAttributes(classHandle, attributes);
diagnostics.recordObjectClass(className, true, gotAttributes, missingAttributes);
logger.info("Subscribed {} ({} of {} attributes; not in this FOM: {}).",
className, gotAttributes.size(), attributeNames.size(),
missingAttributes.isEmpty() ? "none" : missingAttributes);
} catch (Exception e) {
diagnostics.recordObjectClass(className, false, List.of(), attributeNames);
logger.warn("Could not subscribe object class {}: {}", className, e.getMessage());
}
}
// ── Time management ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
private void enableTimeManagement() throws Exception {
if (!timeManagementEnabled) {
// Deliberately off by default. This bridge is a passive observer
// plus an out-of-band tasking channel: it never requests a time
// advance. A federate that is time-regulating but never advances
// stalls every time-constrained federate in the federation — it
// would freeze SWORD/VBS4 rather than just the bridge. Without time
// management, everything is sent/received in receive order, which
// is what this bridge wants. Set BRIDGE_TIME_MANAGEMENT=true only
// alongside a real time-advance loop.
logger.info("Time management disabled (receive-order only). "
+ "Set BRIDGE_TIME_MANAGEMENT=true to enable.");
return;
}
logger.info("Enabling time management...");
HLAfloat64TimeFactory timeFactory =
(HLAfloat64TimeFactory) rtiAmbassador.getTimeFactory();
HLAfloat64Interval lookahead = timeFactory.makeInterval(1.0);
try {
rtiAmbassador.enableTimeConstrained();
} catch (TimeConstrainedAlreadyEnabled e) {
logger.debug("Time constrained already enabled.");
}
try {
rtiAmbassador.enableTimeRegulation(lookahead);
} catch (TimeRegulationAlreadyEnabled e) {
logger.debug("Time regulation already enabled.");
}
}
// ── Interaction sending ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
/**
* Look up a previously resolved interaction class handle.
*
* @throws IllegalStateException if not connected, or if the class is not
*         present in the loaded FOM
*/
public InteractionClassHandle interaction(String fomClassName) {
if (rtiAmbassador == null) {
throw new IllegalStateException("Not connected to the RTI yet.");
}
InteractionClassHandle handle = interactionHandles.get(fomClassName);
if (handle == null) {
throw new IllegalStateException(
"Interaction class not available in the loaded FOM: " + fomClassName);
}
return handle;
}
public boolean hasInteraction(String fomClassName) {
return interactionHandles.containsKey(fomClassName);
}
public ParameterHandle parameter(InteractionClassHandle handle, String parameterName) throws Exception {
return rtiAmbassador.getParameterHandle(handle, parameterName);
}
public ParameterHandleValueMap newParameterMap(int capacity) throws Exception {
return rtiAmbassador.getParameterHandleValueMapFactory().create(capacity);
}
public void sendInteraction(InteractionClassHandle handle,
ParameterHandleValueMap params) throws Exception {
if (rtiAmbassador == null) {
throw new IllegalStateException("RTI Ambassador not initialised.");
}
rtiAmbassador.sendInteraction(handle, params, null);
}
// ── Attribute update ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
public void updateAttributeValues(ObjectInstanceHandle instanceHandle,
AttributeHandleValueMap attributes) throws Exception {
if (rtiAmbassador == null) {
throw new IllegalStateException("RTI Ambassador not initialised.");
}
rtiAmbassador.updateAttributeValues(instanceHandle, attributes, null);
}
// ── Disconnect ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
/** How long to allow for a clean resign before giving up and exiting. */
private static final int SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10;
/**
* Resign and close the FedPro session when the container stops.
*
* <p>{@code @PreDestroy} is the point of this method. Without it the JVM was
* simply killed with the federate still joined and the session still open,
* and an abandoned session is what makes the FedPro server allocate a new LRC
* port next time — so the advertised address climbs 6101, 6102, 6103 ... and
* the CRC starts refusing joins because it cannot verify an address it has
* already seen registered. Every restart made the next start worse. Spring
* Boot installs a JVM shutdown hook by default, so a SIGTERM closes the
* context and reaches this method.
*
* <p>The bounded, best-effort mechanics live in {@link #resignAndClose(String)},
* which {@link #releaseSlot()} shares.
*/
@PreDestroy
public void disconnect() {
running = false;
resignAndClose("Shutting down");
}
/**
* Resign on request, leaving this process running so it can rejoin later.
*
* <p>Exists because licensed pRTI federate seats are scarce and an unclean
* container stop leaks one for {@code FED_TIMEOUT_RECONNECT} — ten minutes on
* CSE-Prep — after which the next start is pushed onto a suffixed name and
* holds a second seat. Two bridges restarted uncleanly is four seats. Calling
* this before stopping the module releases the seat deterministically instead
* of depending on SIGTERM reaching the JVM before SIGKILL does.
*
* <p>Deliberately does <em>not</em> clear {@code running}: the run loop has to
* stay alive to service a later {@link #requestRejoin()}. The loop tolerates a
* null ambassador — it sleeps, and its attribute refresh is already guarded on
* the federation ambassador being present.
*
* @return false if nothing was joined, so there was no seat to release
*/
public boolean releaseSlot() {
return resignAndClose("Releasing the federate slot on request");
}
/**
* Ask the run loop to connect and join again after {@link #releaseSlot()}.
*
* <p>Sets the same flag {@link #onConnectionLost()} uses, so the rejoin runs on
* the run loop thread rather than the caller's HTTP thread — re-entering the
* RTI from anywhere else is exactly what {@code onConnectionLost} exists to
* avoid. Returns as soon as the flag is set; poll {@code GET /status} for the
* outcome.
*
* @return false if already connected, or if the run loop is not running
*/
public boolean requestRejoin() {
if (rtiAmbassador != null || !running) {
return false;
}
reconnectRequested = true;
return true;
}
/**
* Resign and close the FedPro session. Shared by shutdown and slot release.
*
* <p>Bounded on purpose. If the RTI is unreachable the resign can hang, and
* hanging until the pod's termination grace period expires earns a SIGKILL —
* which leaks the session anyway, the precise outcome this is here to
* prevent. Better to try for a few seconds and exit cleanly.
*
* @return false if there was no live ambassador to resign
*/
private boolean resignAndClose(String reason) {
RTIambassador ambassador = rtiAmbassador;
if (ambassador == null) {
return false;
}
logger.info("{}: resigning from '{}' so the FedPro session is closed rather "
+ "than abandoned.", reason, federationName);
java.util.concurrent.ExecutorService closer =
java.util.concurrent.Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor(runnable -> {
Thread thread = new Thread(runnable, "hla-bridge-resign");
thread.setDaemon(true);
return thread;
});
try {
closer.submit(() -> {
try {
ambassador.resignFederationExecution(
ResignAction.DELETE_OBJECTS_THEN_DIVEST);
logger.info("Resigned from federation '{}'.", federationName);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.warn("Resign failed, still attempting to close the session: {}",
e.getMessage());
}
try {
ambassador.disconnect();
logger.info("FedPro session closed cleanly.");
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.warn("Could not close the FedPro session: {}", e.getMessage());
}
return null;
}).get(SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS, java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit.SECONDS);
} catch (java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException e) {
logger.warn("Resign did not complete within {}s; exiting anyway. The session may be "
+ "left open, and the next start will be given a new LRC port.",
SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS);
} catch (Exception e) {
logger.warn("Error while shutting down: {}", e.getMessage());
} finally {
closer.shutdownNow();
rtiAmbassador = null;
federationAmbassador = null;
interactionHandles.clear();
dialect = null;
store.setConnected(false);
}
return true;
}
// ── Getters ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
public RTIambassador getRtiAmbassador()                         { return rtiAmbassador; }
public String getCrcAddress()                                   { return crcAddress; }
/** The name we asked to join as. */
public String getPreferredFederateName()                        { return preferredFederateName; }
/** The name we actually joined as, or null before the first successful join. */
public String getFederateName()                                 { return federateName; }
public boolean isMonitorOnly()                                  { return monitorOnly; }
/** Whether this bridge created the execution it is joined to — see the field. */
public boolean isFederationCreatedByUs()                        { return federationCreatedByUs; }
/**
* The CRC candidate that answered, or null before a successful connect.
*
* <p>Worth reporting: the candidate list exists because the CRC's address is
* not stable here, each instance learns its own, and two bridges that picked
* different ones are attached to different RTIs — and so to two different
* federations that share a name.
*/
public String getWorkingCrcCandidate()                          { return workingCrcCandidate; }
public Map<ObjectInstanceHandle, Entity> getDiscoveredEntities(){ return discoveredEntities; }
/**
* The tasking dialect in use.
*
* @throws IllegalStateException if not connected yet, or if this federation
*         defines neither dialect. Both are mapped to HTTP 409 by
*         {@code BridgeServer}, with the message explaining which.
*/
public TaskDialect dialect() {
TaskDialect current = dialect;
if (current == null) {
if (rtiAmbassador == null) {
throw new IllegalStateException(
"Not connected to the RTI yet, so no tasking dialect has been selected.");
}
throw new TaskDialect.UnsupportedTaskException(
"This federation's FOM does not define " + v4Dialect.detectionClass()
+ ", so there is nothing to task with. Entities can still be monitored. This "
+ "bridge speaks NETN v4 only — if the federation is running a v2-era FOM "
+ "(ETR_Root.ETR_Task.*), it is the wrong model: CSE-Prep runs the merged "
+ "NETN/RPR FOM. Check /diagnostics/fom and which modules the federation was "
+ "created with.");
}
return current;
}
/** The dialect id ("v2"/"v4") or "none", for /status. Never throws. */
public String dialectId() {
TaskDialect current = dialect;
return current == null ? "none" : current.id();
}
/** The dialect display name or "none", for /status. Never throws. */
public String dialectName() {
TaskDialect current = dialect;
return current == null ? "none" : current.name();
}
}

[main/java/com/decho/cse/bridge/BridgeServer.java]
package com.decho.cse.bridge;
import com.decho.cse.bridge.model.Entity;
import com.decho.cse.bridge.model.request.*;
import org.slf4j.Logger;
import org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.http.HttpStatus;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
/**
* REST facade over the HLA federation.
*
* <p>The HTTP contract is unchanged from the previous version (the Foundry
* functions in {@code hla-bridge-functions/app.py} call these paths), but every
* endpoint now sends the correct concrete NETN interaction via
* {@link EtrTaskSender} instead of a generic interaction with a "TaskType"
* string. Tasking responses now also return the {@code taskId}, which is what
* the task status report reports progress against.
*
* <h2>Dialect independence</h2>
* The paths, request bodies and vocabulary here are the same whichever NETN FOM
* version the federation runs: this layer speaks degrees, seconds, latitude and
* longitude, and {@link TaskDialect} converts to whatever the loaded FOM wants.
* Two consequences are visible to callers:
* <ul>
*   <li>Every tasking response carries a {@code dialect} field ({@code v2} or
*       {@code v4}), and {@code GET /status} reports {@code taskDialect}. Worth
*       checking first when a task is accepted but nothing happens.</li>
*   <li>A few request fields only apply to one dialect — see the individual
*       request records. They are ignored with a log line rather than silently,
*       and a request a dialect cannot express at all returns 409 rather than
*       something approximate.</li>
* </ul>
*/
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/bridge")
public class BridgeServer {
private static final Logger logger = LoggerFactory.getLogger(BridgeServer.class);
private final BridgeFederate bridgeFederate;
private final EtrTaskSender tasks;
private final EntityStateStore store;
private final BridgeDiagnostics diagnostics;
private final BridgeApplication.BuildInfo buildInfo;
@Autowired
public BridgeServer(BridgeFederate bridgeFederate, EtrTaskSender tasks, EntityStateStore store,
BridgeDiagnostics diagnostics, BridgeApplication.BuildInfo buildInfo) {
this.bridgeFederate = bridgeFederate;
this.tasks          = tasks;
this.store          = store;
this.diagnostics    = diagnostics;
this.buildInfo      = buildInfo;
}
// ── Health & Status ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@GetMapping("/health")
public ResponseEntity<String> healthCheck() {
if (store.isConnected()) {
return ResponseEntity.ok("Bridge is connected to RTI.");
}
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE)
.body("Bridge is not connected to RTI. Retrying...");
}
@GetMapping("/status")
public ResponseEntity<StatusResponse> getStatus() {
return ResponseEntity.ok(new StatusResponse(
store.isConnected(),
store.getFederationName(),
store.getSimTime(),
store.getAll().size(),
bridgeFederate.dialectId(),
bridgeFederate.dialectName(),
bridgeFederate.getFederateName(),
bridgeFederate.getWorkingCrcCandidate(),
bridgeFederate.isFederationCreatedByUs(),
diagnostics.federateCount(),
buildInfo.version(),
buildInfo.sha(),
buildInfo.builtAt()
));
}
// ── Diagnostics ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// These exist because every hard problem on this bridge so far was only
// diagnosable by reading pod logs and guessing. They are read-only and
// observational: nothing here can change what the bridge does.
/**
* One call that explains the current state, ordered most serious first.
*
* <p>Start here. It answers, in plain terms: are we connected, which tasking
* dialect this federation speaks, which FOM modules are missing, whether any
* entity can actually be tasked, and whether anything has ever acknowledged
* a task we sent.
*/
@GetMapping("/diagnostics")
public ResponseEntity<?> diagnosticsSummary() {
List<Entity> entities = store.getAll();
int withoutUniqueId = 0;
for (Entity entity : entities) {
if (entity.uniqueId == null || entity.uniqueId.isBlank()) {
withoutUniqueId++;
}
}
List<BridgeDiagnostics.Finding> findings = diagnostics.assess(
store.isConnected(), store.getFederationName(), entities.size(), withoutUniqueId);
boolean anyError = findings.stream()
.anyMatch(f -> BridgeDiagnostics.Level.ERROR.name().equals(f.level()));
boolean anyWarning = findings.stream()
.anyMatch(f -> BridgeDiagnostics.Level.WARN.name().equals(f.level()));
// A LinkedHashMap rather than Map.of: this now carries more than the ten
// pairs Map.of accepts, and federateName/crcCandidate are null before a
// successful join — which Map.of rejects outright, turning a diagnostic
// call into a 500 exactly when it is most needed.
Map<String, Object> summary = new LinkedHashMap<>();
summary.put("verdict", anyError ? "BROKEN" : (anyWarning ? "DEGRADED" : "HEALTHY"));
summary.put("findings", findings);
summary.put("connected", store.isConnected());
summary.put("federation", String.valueOf(store.getFederationName()));
summary.put("uptime", diagnostics.uptime());
summary.put("taskDialect", bridgeFederate.dialectId());
summary.put("entities", entities.size());
summary.put("tasksSent", diagnostics.tasksSent());
summary.put("acknowledgementsReceived", diagnostics.acknowledgementsReceived());
// Where this bridge is, as distinct from what it can see. Comparing two
// bridges' entity counts is only meaningful once these three agree.
summary.put("federateName", bridgeFederate.getFederateName());
summary.put("crcCandidate", bridgeFederate.getWorkingCrcCandidate());
summary.put("federationCreatedByUs", bridgeFederate.isFederationCreatedByUs());
summary.put("federateCount", diagnostics.federateCount());
return ResponseEntity.ok(summary);
}
/**
* Every interaction class the bridge knows about: which dialect and FOM
* module it belongs to, whether this federation defines it, and whether we
* publish or subscribe to it.
*
* <p>This is the answer to "what does this federation actually support?",
* which previously required a fomdump of a file that might not be the one the
* RTI loaded.
*/
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/fom")
public ResponseEntity<?> fomClasses() {
List<BridgeDiagnostics.FomClassStatus> all = diagnostics.fomClassStatuses();
long resolved = all.stream().filter(BridgeDiagnostics.FomClassStatus::resolved).count();
// Which endpoint sends which class, under the dialect in use. Absent when
// no dialect was detected, in which case nothing can be sent anyway.
Map<String, String> operations = new LinkedHashMap<>();
try {
for (TaskDialect.Op op : TaskDialect.Op.values()) {
operations.put(op.name(), tasks.classFor(op));
}
} catch (Exception e) {
operations.put("error", e.getMessage());
}
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"taskDialect", bridgeFederate.dialectId(),
"taskDialectName", bridgeFederate.dialectName(),
"knownClasses", all.size(),
"resolvedInThisFederation", resolved,
"operationToClass", operations,
"classes", all));
}
/**
* Which object classes this federation defines, and which attributes of each we
* were able to subscribe to.
*
* <p>The counterpart of {@code /diagnostics/fom}, which covers interactions only.
* This is the answer to "why is this entity field null?", and until now it lived
* exclusively in DEBUG log lines: an attribute the FOM spells differently — NETN
* 2's {@code UniqueID} versus NETN 3's {@code UniqueId} — is skipped without
* comment, and an entity then arrives with a name and no identity. A class listed
* here as {@code resolved: false} is one this federation does not define, so
* anything published as it is discovered at an ancestor instead.
*/
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/objects")
public ResponseEntity<?> objectClasses() {
List<BridgeDiagnostics.ObjectClassStatus> all = diagnostics.objectClassStatuses();
long resolved = all.stream().filter(BridgeDiagnostics.ObjectClassStatus::resolved).count();
Map<String, Object> body = new LinkedHashMap<>();
body.put("subscribedClasses", resolved);
body.put("knownClasses", all.size());
body.put("note", "Attributes under 'missing' are not defined by this federation's FOM and "
+ "were skipped. If an entity has a name but no identity or position, look for the "
+ "class it was published as being unresolved, or its identity attributes missing.");
body.put("objectClasses", all);
return ResponseEntity.ok(body);
}
// ── Federate slot management ──────────────────────────────────────────────
/**
* Resign from the federation, releasing this federate's pRTI licence seat.
*
* <p>Only ever resigns <em>this</em> bridge. HLA offers no way to resign another
* federate, so this cannot release the seat held by HLA-ENTITY-STREAM-READER
* or by a ghost left behind by a previous run — those need a clean stop of
* their own, or an admin eviction from the pRTI console.
*
* <p>The process keeps running and can rejoin via {@code POST /reconnect}, so
* the intended sequence before stopping the compute module is: call this,
* confirm {@code GET /status} reports {@code connected: false}, then stop. That
* releases the seat deterministically rather than relying on SIGTERM reaching
* the JVM ahead of SIGKILL.
*
* <p>Idempotent: resigning when not joined is a 200 with
* {@code released: false}, not an error.
*/
@PostMapping("/disconnect")
public ResponseEntity<?> disconnectFederate() {
try {
boolean released = bridgeFederate.releaseSlot();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", released ? "resigned" : "not-joined",
"released", released,
"connected", store.isConnected(),
"note", released
? "Resigned and closed the FedPro session. The licence seat is free. "
+ "The process is still running — POST /reconnect to rejoin."
: "Nothing was joined, so no seat was held. Already released, or the "
+ "bridge never connected."));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("resign from the federation", e);
}
}
/**
* Connect and join again after {@code POST /disconnect}.
*
* <p>Asynchronous by design: the rejoin is performed on the run loop thread,
* because re-entering the RTI from an HTTP thread is unsafe for the same reason
* {@code FederationAmbassador.connectionLost} defers to that loop. This returns
* as soon as the request is queued — poll {@code GET /status} until
* {@code connected} is true.
*
* <p>The federate name is resolved fresh, so if a ghost still holds the
* preferred name this rejoin will land on a suffixed one. Wait out
* FED_TIMEOUT_RECONNECT if the original name matters.
*/
@PostMapping("/reconnect")
public ResponseEntity<?> reconnectFederate() {
try {
boolean queued = bridgeFederate.requestRejoin();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", queued ? "rejoin-requested" : "no-action",
"queued", queued,
"connected", store.isConnected(),
"note", queued
? "The run loop will connect and join within about a second. Poll "
+ "GET /status until connected is true."
: store.isConnected()
? "Already connected; nothing to do."
: "The run loop is not running, so it cannot rejoin. Restart "
+ "the container."));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("rejoin the federation", e);
}
}
/**
* The federates joined to this federation, from the RTI's own MOM data.
*
* <p>{@code objectsRegistered} and {@code updatesSent} are the useful pair: a
* federate registering objects and sending updates while {@code /entities} is
* empty means a subscription gap on our side, not a publishing problem on
* theirs.
*/
/**
* Who is publishing entities on the DIS side, grouped by site/application.
*
* <p>A federate's DIS site/application pair is effectively its address, so
* grouping live entities by it answers "which of these are MACE, and which
* are VBS4?" — a question nothing else in this bridge can answer, because
* {@code simulator} is inferred from instance names and is unreliable
* (SWORD-named entities have turned up in the vbs4 bucket).
*
* <p>This is the gate for any RPR-addressed control path: a SetData datum is
* addressed to an EntityIdentifier, so until we can name a MACE or VBS4
* entity as site-app-entity there is nothing to send one to.
*/
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/dis-sources")
public ResponseEntity<?> disSources() {
List<Map<String, Object>> sources = store.observedDisSourcesGrouped();
int addressable = store.getRprAddressable().size();
int total       = store.getAll().size();
Map<String, Object> body = new LinkedHashMap<>();
body.put("sources", sources);
body.put("entitiesWithEntityIdentifier", addressable);
body.put("entitiesTotal", total);
if (sources.isEmpty()) {
body.put("note", total == 0
? "No entities discovered yet."
: "No entity has reflected an EntityIdentifier. Either no publisher sends it, "
+ "or it is not subscribed at the class these entities were discovered at — "
+ "see /diagnostics/objects.");
}
return ResponseEntity.ok(body);
}
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/federates")
public ResponseEntity<?> federates() {
List<BridgeDiagnostics.FederateRecord> all = diagnostics.federateRecords();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"count", all.size(),
"note", all.isEmpty()
? "No MOM data reported. Either the RTI does not expose HLAmanager, or it "
+ "has not answered the attribute request yet."
: "From HLAobjectRoot.HLAmanager.HLAfederate, published by the RTI itself.",
"federates", all));
}
/**
* Re-ask the RTI for MOM federate details.
*
* <p>The automatic request happens once at connect, so a simulator started
* after the bridge does not appear until asked for again. The RTI answers
* asynchronously: call this, then read {@code /diagnostics/federates}.
*/
@PostMapping("/diagnostics/refresh-federates")
public ResponseEntity<?> refreshFederates() {
if (!store.isConnected()) {
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE)
.body(Map.of("error", "Not connected to the RTI."));
}
bridgeFederate.refreshFederateReports();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "requested",
"note", "The RTI replies asynchronously. Read /diagnostics/federates in a moment.",
"knownNow", diagnostics.federateRecords().size()));
}
/**
* Recent tasks and whether anything answered them.
*
* <p>The distinction this endpoint exists for: a 200 from a tasking endpoint
* means the interaction was sent. An entry here with an empty
* {@code acknowledgements} list means nothing has replied — which, on a FOM
* that defines a task status class, is the signal that no federate is
* listening.
*/
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/tasks")
public ResponseEntity<?> taskHistory() {
List<BridgeDiagnostics.TaskRecord> all = diagnostics.recentTasks();
long acknowledged = all.stream()
.filter(BridgeDiagnostics.TaskRecord::isAcknowledged).count();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"sentThisSession", diagnostics.tasksSent(),
"inHistory", all.size(),
"acknowledged", acknowledged,
"unacknowledged", all.size() - acknowledged,
"tasks", all));
}
/**
* Exactly what {@code POST /entities/{id}/move} would send, without sending it.
*
* <p>For reconciling with another federate's decoder. When two teams implement
* {@code MoveToLocationTaskStruct} independently from the same spec, the
* failure mode is not an error: both sides are confident and one of them is
* reading geocentric metres as degrees. This returns the field values, their
* datatypes and the encoded bytes, which settles it in one message.
*
* <p>Defaults match the move endpoint, so the output describes what a real
* call with the same arguments would produce.
*/
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/preview/move-task")
public ResponseEntity<?> previewMoveTask(
@RequestParam double lat,
@RequestParam double lon,
@RequestParam(required = false) Double alt,
@RequestParam(required = false) String moveType,
@RequestParam(required = false) Double speedMetersPerSecond) {
try {
Map<String, String> fields = tasks.describeMoveTask(
lat, lon, alt == null ? 0.0 : alt, moveType, speedMetersPerSecond);
Map<String, Object> body = new LinkedHashMap<>();
body.put("dialect", tasks.dialectId());
body.put("sent", false);
body.put("note", "Nothing was sent. This is what a move with these arguments would "
+ "put on the wire. Compare the Location datatype and bytes with what the "
+ "receiving federate decodes.");
body.put("wouldSend", fields);
return ResponseEntity.ok(body);
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("preview a move task", e);
}
}
/** Interaction classes actually received, with counts and last-seen times. */
@GetMapping("/diagnostics/interactions")
public ResponseEntity<?> interactionTraffic() {
List<BridgeDiagnostics.InteractionTraffic> all = diagnostics.interactionTraffic();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"distinctClasses", all.size(),
"note", all.isEmpty()
? "Nothing received. Normal in a quiet scenario — entity position updates "
+ "are attribute reflections, not interactions."
: "Counted since the current connection was established.",
"received", all));
}
/**
* Can this specific entity be tasked, and with what.
*
* <p>Collects the three things that decide it — a NETN UniqueId to address,
* a tasking dialect in the FOM, and the entity's own SupportedActions — plus
* any tasks already sent to it and their fate.
*/
@GetMapping("/entities/{id}/diagnostics")
public ResponseEntity<?> entityDiagnostics(@PathVariable String id) {
Entity entity = store.get(id);
if (entity == null) {
entity = store.getByUniqueId(id).orElse(null);
}
if (entity == null) {
// Third way to name an entity, and the only one available to a
// simulator that publishes no NETN UniqueId: its DIS
// EntityIdentifier, written "site-app-entity".
entity = store.getByEntityIdentifier(id).orElse(null);
}
if (entity == null) {
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND).body(Map.of(
"error", "Entity not found: " + id,
"note", "Tasking still accepts a bare UUID for an entity this bridge has not "
+ "discovered — HLA is decoupled, and an entity can exist without us "
+ "subscribing to the class it was published as."));
}
List<String> blockers = new ArrayList<>();
boolean hasUniqueId = entity.uniqueId != null && !entity.uniqueId.isBlank();
// Addressable on the DIS side even with no NETN identity — which is the
// expected shape for MACE and VBS4, and the precondition for any
// RPR-addressed control path.
boolean rprAddressable = entity.entityIdentifier != null;
if (!hasUniqueId) {
blockers.add(rprAddressable
? "No NETN UniqueId: NETN tasks address entities by UUID, so this entity "
+ "cannot be tasked through a NETN dialect. It does carry a DIS "
+ "EntityIdentifier (" + entity.entityIdentifier + "), so it is addressable "
+ "by any RPR-addressed path."
: "No NETN UniqueId and no DIS EntityIdentifier: this entity cannot be "
+ "addressed by any route. Check which class it was discovered at — only "
+ "attributes available there can reach us.");
}
String dialect = bridgeFederate.dialectId();
if ("none".equals(dialect) || "unknown".equals(dialect)) {
blockers.add("This federation's FOM defines no NETN tasking classes.");
}
List<String> supported = diagnostics.supportedActionsFor(entity.id).orElse(null);
if (supported != null && supported.isEmpty()) {
blockers.add("The entity advertises SupportedActions but the list is empty: its "
+ "simulator accepts no NETN control actions, so it cannot be tasked.");
}
List<BridgeDiagnostics.TaskRecord> sentToThis = new ArrayList<>();
for (BridgeDiagnostics.TaskRecord record : diagnostics.recentTasks()) {
if (record.getEntity() != null
&& (record.getEntity().equals(entity.uniqueId) || record.getEntity().equals(id))) {
sentToThis.add(record);
}
}
Map<String, Object> body = new LinkedHashMap<>();
body.put("id", entity.id);
body.put("uniqueId", String.valueOf(entity.uniqueId));
// The DIS-side identity, reported whether or not a NETN one exists. For
// MACE and VBS4 this is expected to be the only name an entity has.
body.put("entityIdentifier", entity.entityIdentifier == null
? "not reflected (EntityIdentifier absent or not subscribed at the discovered class)"
: entity.entityIdentifier);
body.put("disSiteId", entity.disSiteId);
body.put("disApplicationId", entity.disApplicationId);
body.put("disEntityNumber", entity.disEntityNumber);
body.put("rprAddressable", rprAddressable);
body.put("name", String.valueOf(entity.name));
body.put("entityType", String.valueOf(entity.entityType));
body.put("lastUpdated", entity.lastUpdated);
body.put("position", entity.latitude == null ? "unknown"
: entity.latitude + ", " + entity.longitude);
body.put("taskDialect", dialect);
body.put("supportedActions", supported == null
? "not published by this entity (expected on a v2-era FOM)" : supported);
body.put("taskable", blockers.isEmpty());
body.put("blockers", blockers);
body.put("tasksSentToThisEntity", sentToThis);
return ResponseEntity.ok(body);
}
// ── Entity Queries ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@GetMapping("/entities")
public ResponseEntity<List<Entity>> getEntities() {
return ResponseEntity.ok(store.getAll());
}
@GetMapping("/entities/{id}")
public ResponseEntity<?> getEntity(@PathVariable String id) {
Entity e = store.get(id);
if (e == null) {
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND)
.body(Map.of("error", "Entity not found: " + id));
}
return ResponseEntity.ok(e);
}
@GetMapping("/entities/simulator/{simulator}")
public ResponseEntity<List<Entity>> getEntitiesBySimulator(
@PathVariable String simulator) {
return ResponseEntity.ok(store.getBySimulator(simulator));
}
@GetMapping("/entities/area")
public ResponseEntity<List<Entity>> getEntitiesInArea(
@RequestParam double lat,
@RequestParam double lon,
@RequestParam double radiusKm) {
return ResponseEntity.ok(store.getInArea(lat, lon, radiusKm));
}
// ── Scenario Control ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// RPR-FOM simulation management. There is no NETN "start/pause/stop
// scenario" interaction; StartResume and StopFreeze are the DIS-derived
// federation-wide equivalents.
@PostMapping("/start")
public ResponseEntity<?> startScenario() {
try {
long requestId = tasks.startResume();
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "started",
"interaction", "StartResume",
"requestId", requestId));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("start scenario", e);
}
}
@PostMapping("/pause")
public ResponseEntity<?> pauseScenario() {
try {
long requestId = tasks.stopFreeze(true);
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "paused",
"interaction", "StopFreeze(Recess)",
"requestId", requestId));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("pause scenario", e);
}
}
@PostMapping("/stop")
public ResponseEntity<?> stopScenario() {
try {
long requestId = tasks.stopFreeze(false);
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "stopped",
"interaction", "StopFreeze(Termination)",
"requestId", requestId));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("stop scenario", e);
}
}
// ── Movement ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/move")
public ResponseEntity<?> moveToLocation(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody MoveRequest req) {
try {
Taskee taskee = resolveTaskee(id);
String uuid = taskee.uuid();
var taskId = tasks.moveToLocation(uuid, req.latitude(), req.longitude(),
altitudeOf(req.altitudeMeters(), taskee.entity()), req.moveType(),
req.speedMetersPerSecond(),
composeAnnotation(req.annotation(), req.orbitRadiusMeters()));
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "tasked", "entityId", id, "task", "MoveToLocation",
"taskId", taskId.toString(), "dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("send move task for " + id, e);
}
}
@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/patrol")
public ResponseEntity<?> patrol(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody PatrolRequest req) {
try {
Taskee taskee = resolveTaskee(id);
String uuid = taskee.uuid();
if (req.waypoints() == null || req.waypoints().isEmpty()) {
throw new IllegalArgumentException("At least one waypoint is required.");
}
List<double[]> path = new ArrayList<>();
for (PatrolRequest.Waypoint wp : req.waypoints()) {
path.add(new double[]{wp.latitude(), wp.longitude(),
wp.altitudeMeters() == null ? 0.0 : wp.altitudeMeters()});
}
var taskId = tasks.patrol(uuid, path, req.patrolType(), req.moveType(),
req.durationSeconds(), req.intervalSeconds(), req.speedMetersPerSecond());
boolean repeating = req.durationSeconds() != null && req.durationSeconds() > 0;
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "tasked", "entityId", id,
// v4 merged PatrolRepeating into Patrol; v2 uses a subclass.
"task", repeating ? taskLabel("PatrolRepeating", "Patrol") : "Patrol",
"repeating", repeating,
"taskId", taskId.toString(), "dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("send patrol task for " + id, e);
}
}
@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/magic-move")
public ResponseEntity<?> magicMove(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody MagicMoveRequest req) {
try {
Taskee taskee = resolveTaskee(id);
String uuid = taskee.uuid();
var taskId = tasks.magicMove(uuid, req.latitude(), req.longitude(),
altitudeOf(req.altitudeMeters(), taskee.entity()), req.headingDegrees());
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "moved", "entityId", id, "task", "MagicMove",
"taskId", taskId.toString(), "dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("magic-move entity " + id, e);
}
}
// ── Fires ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/fire-at-location")
public ResponseEntity<?> fireAtLocation(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody FireAtLocationRequest req) {
try {
Taskee taskee = resolveTaskee(id);
String uuid = taskee.uuid();
var taskId = tasks.fireAtLocation(uuid, req.latitude(), req.longitude(),
req.altitudeMeters() == null ? 0.0 : req.altitudeMeters(),
req.durationSeconds());
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "tasked", "entityId", id,
"task", taskLabel("FireAtLocation", "IndirectFire"),
"taskId", taskId.toString(), "dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("send fire-at-location for " + id, e);
}
}
@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/fire-at-entity")
public ResponseEntity<?> fireAtEntity(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody FireAtEntityRequest req) {
try {
String attackerUuid = resolveTaskee(id).uuid();
String targetUuid = resolveTaskee(req.targetId()).uuid();
var taskId = tasks.fireAtEntity(attackerUuid, targetUuid);
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "tasked", "entityId", id,
"targetId", String.valueOf(req.targetId()),
"task", taskLabel("FireAtEntity", "DirectFire"),
"taskId", taskId.toString(), "dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("send fire-at-entity for " + id, e);
}
}

@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/disaggregate")
public ResponseEntity<?> disaggregate(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody(required = false) MrmRequest req) {

}
@PostMapping("/entities/{id}/aggregate")
public ResponseEntity<?> aggregate(@PathVariable String id,
@RequestBody(required = false) MrmRequest req) {
return multiResolution(id, req, true);
}
private ResponseEntity<?> multiResolution(String id, MrmRequest req, boolean aggregate) {
try {
Taskee taskee = resolveTaskee(id);
String uuid = taskee.uuid();
boolean removeSubunits = req != null && Boolean.TRUE.equals(req.removeSubunits());
var eventId = tasks.multiResolution(aggregate, uuid,
req == null ? null : req.federate(), removeSubunits);
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", aggregate ? "aggregating" : "disaggregating",
"entityId", id,
"interaction", aggregate ? "MRM Aggregate" : "MRM Disaggregate",
"eventId", eventId.toString(),
"dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure((aggregate ? "aggregate" : "disaggregate") + " entity " + id, e);
}
}

@PostMapping("/map/obstacle")
public ResponseEntity<?> createObstacle(@RequestBody ObstacleRequest req) {
try {
if (req.entityId() == null || req.entityId().isBlank()) {
throw new IllegalArgumentException(
"CreateObstacle is an ETR task and requires an engineer entity to task: "
+ "provide \"entityId\".");
}
Taskee taskee = resolveTaskee(req.entityId());
String uuid = taskee.uuid();
double radius = req.radiusMeters() == null ? 50.0 : req.radiusMeters();
var obstacleUuid = tasks.createObstacle(uuid, req.type(), req.obstacleType(),
req.latitude(), req.longitude(), radius);
return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
"status", "created", "type", String.valueOf(req.type()),
"entityId", req.entityId(), "obstacleId", obstacleUuid.toString(),
"dialect", tasks.dialectId()));
} catch (Exception e) {
return failure("create obstacle", e);
}
}

// ── Helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
/**
* The resolved target of a task: the UUID to put in the ETR {@code Taskee}
* parameter, plus the discovered entity if we happen to know it.
*
* @param entity null when the entity has not been discovered by this bridge
*/
private record Taskee(String uuid, Entity entity) {}
/**
* Resolve a request's entity identifier to the NETN {@code UniqueId} used as
* the ETR {@code Taskee}.
*
* <p>Accepts an HLA object-instance handle or a NETN UniqueId of a
* discovered entity, and also a bare UUID for an entity this bridge has not
* discovered. That last case is deliberate: HLA is decoupled, and an entity
* existing in the federation is independent of whether we happen to
* subscribe to the class it was published as. Refusing to address a
* perfectly valid UUID just because we have not seen the object would make
* the bridge stricter than the federation it is talking to, and makes the
* send path impossible to exercise before any publisher exists.
*
* <p>An earlier version looked the entity up first and threw 404 before
* reaching this fallback, which made the fallback unreachable.
*/
private Taskee resolveTaskee(String id) throws EntityNotFoundException {
Entity entity = store.get(id);
if (entity == null) {
entity = store.getByUniqueId(id).orElse(null);
}
if (entity != null && entity.uniqueId != null && !entity.uniqueId.isBlank()) {
return new Taskee(entity.uniqueId, entity);
}
// Either not discovered, or discovered without a NETN UniqueId. Both are
// usable if the caller gave us a UUID to address.
try {
String uuid = HlaEncoder.parseUuid(id).toString();
if (entity == null) {
logger.warn("Entity {} has not been discovered by this bridge; tasking it by UUID "
+ "anyway. Watch for an ETR TaskStatusReport to see whether any federate "
+ "accepts it.", id);
} else {
logger.warn("Entity {} was discovered without a NETN UniqueId; using the supplied "
+ "UUID as the Taskee.", id);
}
return new Taskee(uuid, entity);
} catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {
if (entity == null) {
// Not a UUID and not known: nothing we can address.
throw new EntityNotFoundException(id);
}
throw new IllegalStateException(
"Entity '" + id + "' has no NETN UniqueId, so it cannot be tasked. ETR tasks "
+ "address entities by UUID. Either pass the UUID directly, or check that the "
+ "entity is published as a NETN_* object class with the UniqueId attribute.");
}
}
/**
* The FOM class name a task was actually sent as, for the response body.
*
* <p>Several tasks were renamed in v4 (FireAtLocation to IndirectFire, and
* so on). Reporting the name that went on the wire means the response can be
* matched against an RTI trace or the simulator's own log without first
* working out which dialect was in play.
*/
private String taskLabel(String v2Name, String v4Name) {
return "v4".equals(tasks.dialectId()) ? v4Name : v2Name;
}
/**
* Build the task Annotation, optionally carrying an orbit radius.
*
* <p>NETN-ETR has no orbit-radius field. The CSE-Prep drone mockup therefore
* reads {@code range=<metres>} (and {@code alt=<metres>}) out of the
* Annotation text, which is the only channel the FOM leaves for it. This is a
* convention of that federate rather than part of the standard, so it is
* emitted only when a caller asks for it, and appended to whatever note they
* supplied rather than replacing it. A receiver that does not look for the
* key simply sees a human-readable annotation, which is what NETN intends.
*
* <p>Formatted as a plain decimal with no exponent, because the receiver
* matches on {@code (\w+)\s*=\s*(-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?)} — {@code 1.5E4} would not
* match.
*/
private static String composeAnnotation(String annotation, Double orbitRadiusMeters) {
String text = annotation == null || annotation.isBlank()
? "CSE bridge move order" : annotation.trim();
if (orbitRadiusMeters == null || orbitRadiusMeters <= 0) {
return text;
}
return text + " range=" + new java.math.BigDecimal(orbitRadiusMeters)
.setScale(1, java.math.RoundingMode.HALF_UP).toPlainString();
}
private static double altitudeOf(Double requested, Entity entity) {
if (requested != null) return requested;
if (entity != null && entity.altitudeMeters != null) return entity.altitudeMeters;
return 0.0;
}
/**
* Map failures onto sensible HTTP statuses:
* 404 unknown entity, 400 bad request, 409 not taskable, 503 RTI problems.
*
* <p>409 covers three related cases, all "this federation cannot do that":
* the interaction class is absent from the loaded FOM, no NETN tasking
* dialect was detected at all, or the live dialect cannot express the
* request ({@link TaskDialect.UnsupportedTaskException}). The message says
* which.
*/
private ResponseEntity<?> failure(String action, Exception e) {
if (e instanceof EntityNotFoundException) {
logger.info("Could not {}: {}", action, e.getMessage());
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND)
.body(Map.of("error", e.getMessage()));
}
if (e instanceof IllegalArgumentException) {
logger.info("Could not {}: {}", action, e.getMessage());
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST)
.body(Map.of("error", e.getMessage()));
}
if (e instanceof IllegalStateException) {
logger.warn("Could not {}: {}", action, e.getMessage());
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.CONFLICT)
.body(Map.of("error", e.getMessage()));
}
logger.error("Failed to {}", action, e);
return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE)
.body(Map.of("error", "RTI error: " + e.getMessage()));
}
// ── DTOs ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
/**
* @param taskDialect     "v2", "v4" or "none" — which NETN tasking dialect
*                        this federation's FOM defines. "none" means
*                        monitoring works but every tasking endpoint will
*                        return 409.
* @param taskDialectName the human-readable form of the same thing
* @param federateName    the name this bridge actually joined under, which is
*                        not always the configured one — a suffix is appended
*                        when the name is already registered
* @param crcCandidate    the CRC candidate that answered. Two bridges with the
*                        same configuration can pick different ones, and are
*                        then attached to different federations that share a
*                        name — so this is the first field to compare when one
*                        bridge sees entities and another does not
* @param federationCreatedByUs whether this bridge created the execution
*                        rather than joining one. True means every other
*                        federate is elsewhere and nothing will ever be
*                        discovered here
* @param federateCount   federates known from the RTI's MOM, including this
*                        bridge. 1 means it is alone; 0 means the RTI has not
*                        reported yet, which is not the same thing
*/
public record StatusResponse(
boolean connected,
String federationName,
double simTime,
int entityCount,
String taskDialect,
String taskDialectName,
String federateName,
String crcCandidate,
boolean federationCreatedByUs,
int federateCount,
/** Version of the running jar. */
String buildVersion,
/**
* The commit this jar was built from, or "unknown" if the image was not
* built through the project Dockerfile. Reported because a bridge whose
* behaviour matches no commit cannot be reasoned about from the source.
*/
String buildSha,
String builtAt
) {}
/** Internal exception for entity lookup failures. */
private static class EntityNotFoundException extends Exception {
EntityNotFoundException(String id) {
super("Entity not found: " + id);
}
}
}

[main/java/com/decho/cse/bridge/EntityStateStore.java]
package com.decho.cse.bridge;
import com.decho.cse.bridge.model.Entity;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import java.util.*;
import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;
/**
* Thread-safe in-memory store of all live HLA entities.
* Written by the FederationAmbassador (HLA callbacks thread).
* Read by BridgeServer (REST API thread).
*/
@Component
public class EntityStateStore {
private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, Entity> entities = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
private volatile double simTime = 0.0;
private volatile boolean connected = false;
private volatile String federationName = "";
// ── Write (called from HLA callback thread) ───────────────────────────
public void discover(String id, String name, String entityType) {
discover(id, name, entityType, null);
}
/**
* Record a discovered instance, keeping the FOM class it was discovered at.
*
* <p>The class name is the difference between a diagnosable missing attribute and
* a guess: only attributes available at the discovered class can reach us, so an
* entity with no position is explained by its class, not by its emptiness.
*/
public void discover(String id, String name, String entityType, String discoveredClass) {
entities.computeIfAbsent(id, k -> {
Entity created = new Entity(id, name, entityType, discoveredClass);
// Set at discovery, from the object instance name, which is the only
// publisher-identifying thing available at this point. Previously this
// was derived from the DIS entity kind once EntityType arrived — see
// inferSimulator — which meant it was both wrong and absent until the
// publisher happened to send an attribute.
created.simulator = inferSimulator(name);
return created;
});
}
public void updatePosition(String id, double lat, double lon, double alt) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.latitude       = lat;
e.longitude      = lon;
e.altitudeMeters = alt;
e.touch();
}
}
/**
* Speed in metres per second, from the magnitude of the Spatial velocity vector.
*
* <p>Only present on dead-reckoned Spatial alternatives — a Static entity carries
* no velocity and keeps a null speed, which is correct rather than zero.
*/
public void updateSpeed(String id, double speedMps) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.speedMps = speedMps;
e.touch();
}
}
/** Heading in degrees true north, decoded from the Spatial orientation. */
public void updateHeading(String id, double headingDegrees) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.headingDegrees = headingDegrees;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateEntityType(String id, String dis) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.dis = dis;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateMarking(String id, String marking) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
if (e.name == null || e.name.isBlank()) e.name = marking;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateCallsign(String id, String callsign) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.callsign = callsign;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateSymbol(String id, String symbolId) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.symbolId = symbolId;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateDamageState(String id, String damageState) {
Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.damageState = damageState;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateForce(String id, String forceId) {

if (e != null) {
e.forceId = forceId;
e.touch();
}
}

if (e != null) {
e.aggregateState = aggregateState;
e.touch();
}

if (e != null) {
e.posture = posture;
e.touch();
}

if (e != null) {
e.mission = mission;
e.touch();
}
}

Entity e = entities.get(id);
if (e != null) {
e.activity = activity;
e.touch();
}
}
public void updateCombatValue(String id, int combatValue) {
